```
1	//! `Group` component connect API.
2	//!
3	//! `Group` is a stateless semantic grouping wrapper. The framework-agnostic
4	//! core consists of [`Props`], the [`GroupRole`] role selector, the [`Part`]
5	//! taxonomy, [`Api`], and a [`GroupContext`] struct that adapters publish so
6	//! descendant components can inherit `disabled`, `invalid`, and `read_only`
7	//! state without re-implementing the propagation themselves.
8	//!
9	//! Unlike [`Fieldset`](super::fieldset), which renders the native
10	//! `<fieldset>`/`<legend>` pair and is form-specific, `Group` is a lightweight
11	//! attribute mapper for any related set of controls (a cluster of buttons
12	//! sharing a disabled state, the input + steppers inside a NumberField, etc.).
13	//!
14	//! See `spec/components/utility/group.md` for the authoritative contract.
15	
16	use alloc::string::String;
17	
18	use ars_core::{AriaAttr, AttrMap, ComponentPart, ConnectApi, HasId, HtmlAttr};
19	use ars_i18n::Direction;
20	
21	/// The ARIA role applied to the [`Group`](self) container.
22	///
23	/// Defaults to [`GroupRole::Group`], a generic grouping role suitable for
24	/// related controls. Switch to [`GroupRole::Region`] when the group is a
25	/// landmark that should appear in a page summary (an accessible name is then
26	/// required by WAI-ARIA), or to [`GroupRole::Presentation`] to render the
27	/// container without any grouping semantics while still propagating state via
28	/// [`GroupContext`].
29	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Default)]
30	pub enum GroupRole {
31	    /// `role="group"` — a set of related UI elements not important enough to
32	    /// be included in a page summary or table of contents.
33	    #[default]
34	    Group,
35	    /// `role="region"` — a landmark region that is significant enough to be
36	    /// listed in a page summary. Requires an accessible name.
37	    Region,
38	    /// `role="presentation"` — removes the grouping semantics. Children are
39	    /// still grouped visually but not semantically.
40	    Presentation,
41	}
42	
43	/// Props for the [`Group`](self) component.
44	#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, PartialEq, HasId)]
45	pub struct Props {
46	    /// Component instance ID. Always emitted on the root element so adapters
47	    /// and consumers can reference the container from other attributes
48	    /// (`aria-controls`, `aria-describedby`, etc.).
49	    pub id: String,
50	
51	    /// Whether the group and all contained controls are disabled. Propagated
52	    /// to descendants via [`GroupContext`] and surfaced on the root as
53	    /// `aria-disabled="true"` plus `data-ars-disabled` for styling.
54	    pub disabled: bool,
55	
56	    /// Whether the group is in an invalid state. Propagated to descendants
57	    /// via [`GroupContext`] and surfaced on the root as `aria-invalid="true"`
58	    /// plus `data-ars-invalid` for styling.
59	    pub invalid: bool,
60	
61	    /// Whether the group is read-only. Propagated to descendants via
62	    /// [`GroupContext`] and surfaced on the root as `aria-readonly="true"`
63	    /// plus `data-ars-readonly` for styling.
64	    pub read_only: bool,
65	
66	    /// The ARIA role for the group container. Defaults to [`GroupRole::Group`].
67	    pub role: GroupRole,
68	
69	    /// Layout direction for RTL support. When `Some(_)`, the resolved value
70	    /// is forwarded to the root element's `dir` attribute. When `None`, the
71	    /// element inherits direction from the DOM cascade.
72	    pub dir: Option<Direction>,
73	}
74	
75	impl Props {
76	    /// Returns fresh [`Props`] with the documented defaults — equivalent to
77	    /// [`Default::default`], offered as the entry point for the builder chain.
78	    #[must_use]
79	    pub fn new() -> Self {
80	        Self::default()
81	    }
82	
83	    /// Sets the component instance ID.
84	    #[must_use]
85	    pub fn id(mut self, id: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
86	        self.id = id.into();
87	        self
88	    }
89	
90	    /// Sets whether the group is disabled. Disabled state propagates to
91	    /// descendants through [`GroupContext`].
92	    #[must_use]
93	    pub const fn disabled(mut self, value: bool) -> Self {
94	        self.disabled = value;
95	        self
96	    }
97	
98	    /// Sets whether the group is invalid. Invalid state propagates to
99	    /// descendants through [`GroupContext`].
100	    #[must_use]
101	    pub const fn invalid(mut self, value: bool) -> Self {
102	        self.invalid = value;
103	        self
104	    }
105	
106	    /// Sets whether the group is read-only. Read-only state propagates to
107	    /// descendants through [`GroupContext`].
108	    #[must_use]
109	    pub const fn read_only(mut self, value: bool) -> Self {
110	        self.read_only = value;
111	        self
112	    }
113	
114	    /// Sets the ARIA role of the group container.
115	    #[must_use]
116	    pub const fn role(mut self, value: GroupRole) -> Self {
117	        self.role = value;
118	        self
119	    }
120	
121	    /// Sets the layout direction. Pass `Some(direction)` to forward an
122	    /// explicit `dir` attribute, or `None` to inherit from the DOM cascade.
123	    #[must_use]
124	    pub const fn dir(mut self, value: Option<Direction>) -> Self {
125	        self.dir = value;
126	        self
127	    }
128	}
129	
130	/// Context published by [`Group`](self) so descendant components can inherit
131	/// `disabled`, `invalid`, and `read_only` state without re-implementing the
132	/// propagation themselves.
133	///
134	/// Adapters MUST provide this value through their framework's context system
135	/// (`provide_context` in Leptos, `use_context_provider` in Dioxus). Descendant
136	/// components that participate in inheritance should merge each field with
137	/// their own props using a logical OR — a component is disabled if either it
138	/// or its containing group is disabled.
139	#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
140	pub struct GroupContext {
141	    /// Whether the containing group is disabled.
142	    pub disabled: bool,
143	    /// Whether the containing group is in an invalid state.
144	    pub invalid: bool,
145	    /// Whether the containing group is read-only.
146	    pub read_only: bool,
147	}
148	
149	/// DOM parts of the [`Group`](self) component.
150	#[derive(ComponentPart)]
151	#[scope = "group"]
152	pub enum Part {
153	    /// The root container element. Renders as a `<div>` carrying the role,
154	    /// state attributes, and propagation context.
155	    Root,
156	}
157	
158	/// The API for the [`Group`](self) component.
159	///
160	/// Constructed via [`Api::new`] from [`Props`] and queried via
161	/// [`Api::root_attrs`], [`Api::group_context`], or the [`ConnectApi`] dispatch.
162	#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
163	pub struct Api {
164	    props: Props,
165	}
166	
167	impl Api {
168	    /// Creates a new `Api` instance from the given props.
169	    #[must_use]
170	    pub const fn new(props: Props) -> Self {
171	        Self { props }
172	    }
173	
174	    /// Returns a reference to the underlying [`Props`].
175	    #[must_use]
176	    pub const fn props(&self) -> &Props {
177	        &self.props
178	    }
179	
180	    /// Returns the component's instance ID.
181	    #[must_use]
182	    pub fn id(&self) -> &str {
183	        &self.props.id
184	    }
185	
186	    /// Returns the [`GroupContext`] that adapters should publish to
187	    /// descendants so they can inherit the group's disabled / invalid /
188	    /// read-only state.
189	    #[must_use]
190	    pub const fn group_context(&self) -> GroupContext {
191	        GroupContext {
192	            disabled: self.props.disabled,
193	            invalid: self.props.invalid,
194	            read_only: self.props.read_only,
195	        }
196	    }
197	
198	    /// Returns the attributes for the root element.
199	    ///
200	    /// Always emits the instance `id`, the scope/part data attributes, and a
201	    /// `role` derived from [`Props::role`]. State attributes
202	    /// (`aria-disabled`, `aria-invalid`, `aria-readonly` plus their
203	    /// `data-ars-*` styling counterparts) are emitted whenever the
204	    /// corresponding prop is `true`, regardless of the role — WAI-ARIA 1.2
205	    /// §5.4 preserves global states on `role="presentation"`, and React Aria
206	    /// follows the same convention. When [`Props::dir`] is `Some`, the
207	    /// resolved direction is forwarded to the `dir` attribute.
208	    #[must_use]
209	    pub fn root_attrs(&self) -> AttrMap {
210	        let mut attrs = AttrMap::new();
211	        let [(scope_attr, scope_val), (part_attr, part_val)] = Part::Root.data_attrs();
212	
213	        attrs
214	            .set(HtmlAttr::Id, &self.props.id)
215	            .set(scope_attr, scope_val)
216	            .set(part_attr, part_val);
217	
218	        let role_str = match self.props.role {
219	            GroupRole::Group => "group",
220	            GroupRole::Region => "region",
221	            GroupRole::Presentation => "presentation",
222	        };
223	        attrs.set(HtmlAttr::Role, role_str);
224	
225	        if self.props.disabled {
226	            attrs
227	                .set(HtmlAttr::Aria(AriaAttr::Disabled), "true")
228	                .set_bool(HtmlAttr::Data("ars-disabled"), true);
229	        }
230	        if self.props.invalid {
231	            attrs
232	                .set(HtmlAttr::Aria(AriaAttr::Invalid), "true")
233	                .set_bool(HtmlAttr::Data("ars-invalid"), true);
234	        }
235	        if self.props.read_only {
236	            attrs
237	                .set(HtmlAttr::Aria(AriaAttr::ReadOnly), "true")
238	                .set_bool(HtmlAttr::Data("ars-readonly"), true);
239	        }
240	        if let Some(dir) = self.props.dir {
241	            attrs.set(HtmlAttr::Dir, dir.as_html_attr());
242	        }
243	
244	        attrs
245	    }
246	}
247	
248	impl ConnectApi for Api {
249	    type Part = Part;
250	
251	    fn part_attrs(&self, part: Part) -> AttrMap {
252	        match part {
253	            Part::Root => self.root_attrs(),
254	        }
255	    }
256	}
257

8	
9	# Group — Leptos Adapter
10	
11	## 1. Purpose and Adapter Scope
12	
13	This spec maps the core [`Group`](../../components/utility/group.md) utility to Leptos 0.8.x.
14	
15	## 2. Public Adapter API
16	
17	```rust,no_check
18	#[component] pub fn Group(...) -> impl IntoView
19	```
20	
21	## 3. Mapping to Core Component Contract
22	
23	- Props parity: full parity.
24	- Structure parity: single root grouping element.
25	
26	## 4. Part Mapping
27	
28	| Core part / structure | Required? | Adapter rendering target      | Ownership      | Attr source        | Notes                                                                       |
29	| --------------------- | --------- | ----------------------------- | -------------- | ------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
30	| `Root`                | required  | wrapper `<div>`               | adapter-owned  | `api.root_attrs()` | Carries `role="group"` and inherited state attrs.                           |
31	| children subtree      | required  | consumer children inside root | consumer-owned | none               | May inherit group state through context if a wrapper chooses to provide it. |
32	
33	## 5. Attr Merge and Ownership Rules
34	
35	| Target node                         | Core attrs                                               | Adapter-owned attrs                                                          | Consumer attrs       | Merge order                                                                                                                                        | Ownership notes                                                                                                           |
36	| ----------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
37	| `Root`                              | `api.root_attrs()`                                       | structural `data-*` markers if needed                                        | consumer root attrs  | core group semantics win; `class`/`style` merge additively                                                                                         | adapter-owned root                                                                                                        |
38	| children that consume group context | child utility attrs derived from their own core contract | propagated `disabled`, `invalid`, and `read_only` values from `GroupContext` | consumer child props | child-local core attrs merge with propagated group state; `aria-disabled` on the root alone must not be treated as sufficient descendant disabling | descendant utilities remain consumer-owned but must consume the published group context when they support inherited state |
39	
40	## 6. Composition / Context Contract
41	
42	The adapter publishes `GroupContext` so descendant utilities can merge inherited `disabled`, `invalid`, and `read_only` state with their own props. `aria-disabled` on the root container does not disable descendants by itself; child adapters that participate in group inheritance must read the context and apply the effective state programmatically.
43	
44	## 7. Prop Sync and Event Mapping
45	
46	Group is usually stateless beyond derived props.
47	
48	| Adapter prop             | Mode                      | Sync trigger     | Machine event / update path | Visible effect             | Notes                       |
49	| ------------------------ | ------------------------- | ---------------- | --------------------------- | -------------------------- | --------------------------- |
50	| grouping semantics props | non-reactive adapter prop | render time only | root attr derivation        | updates grouping semantics | no post-mount sync expected |
51	
52	## 8. Registration and Cleanup Contract
53	
54	- No registration lifecycle exists beyond normal root disposal unless a wrapper adds dynamic group context bookkeeping.
55	
56	## 9. Ref and Node Contract
57	
58	| Target part / node                         | Ref required?                                                                          | Ref owner                                             | Node availability                  | Composition rule                                                 | Notes                                                                              |
59	| ------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
60	| primary rendered node or provider boundary | no when the component is purely structural or provider-only; otherwise adapter-defined | adapter-owned unless part mapping says consumer-owned | always structural, handle optional | composed only when a consumer-owned node receives the core attrs | Use a live node handle only when the component's interaction contract requires it. |
61	
62	## 10. State Machine Boundary Rules
63	
64	- machine-owned state: all core interaction, accessibility, and controlled-state values defined by the component machine or derived API.
65	- adapter-local derived bookkeeping: minimal ephemeral data such as pointer modality, mount status, or observer handles when the core machine does not model them directly.
66	- forbidden local mirrors: do not fork controlled props, accessibility attrs, or machine-visible state into an unsynchronized local source of truth.
67	- allowed snapshot-read contexts: render-time derivation, event handlers, effects, and cleanup only when reading snapshots does not bypass required machine events.
68	
69	## 11. Callback Payload Contract
70	
71	| Callback                                                               | Payload source | Payload shape | Timing         | Cancelable? | Notes                                                                                                                 |
72	| ---------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------- | ------------- | -------------- | ----------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
73	| no public adapter-specific callback beyond normalized component events | none           | none          | not applicable | no          | When wrappers expose callbacks, they must preserve the normalized timing documented in `Prop Sync and Event Mapping`. |
74	
75	## 12. Failure and Degradation Rules
76	
77	| Condition                                                                  | Policy             | Notes                                                                             |
78	| -------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
79	| unsupported platform capability or missing browser-only API during SSR     | degrade gracefully | Render structural output and defer behavior until client-only APIs are available. |
80	| impossible prop combinations not explicitly supported by the core contract | fail fast          | Prefer an explicit contract violation over silently inventing behavior.           |
81	
82	## 13. Identity and Key Policy
83	
84	| Registered or repeated structure   | Identity source | Duplicates allowed? | DOM order must match registration order? | SSR/hydration stability                                                  | Notes                                                                                             |
85	| ---------------------------------- | --------------- | ------------------- | ---------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
86	| none beyond the component instance | not applicable  | not applicable      | not applicable                           | stable root structure required where the component renders on the server | Use a component-instance identity only for cleanup bookkeeping when no repeated structure exists. |
87	
88	## 14. SSR and Client Boundary Rules
89	
90	- The server must render every structural node required by the part-mapping table unless the component is explicitly provider-only or client-gated.
91	- Client-only listeners, timers, measurements, and node-handle work must wait until hydration or mount.
92	- Any node that participates in hydration-sensitive semantics must preserve the same structural identity across server and client render paths.
93	
94	## 15. Performance Constraints
95	
96	- Attr maps derived from the machine should be memoized or read through adapter derivation helpers instead of rebuilt eagerly on every render.
97	- Listener, timer, and observer registration must be stable across rerenders and must not churn unless the governing configuration actually changes.
98	- Cleanup must release only the resources owned by the current component instance and must avoid repeated quadratic teardown work.
99	
100	## 16. Implementation Dependencies
101	
102	| Dependency                                  | Required?   | Dependency type | Why it must exist first                                                                                         | Notes                                                                 |
103	| ------------------------------------------- | ----------- | --------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |
104	| none beyond the documented utility contract | recommended | conceptual      | The component may still rely on shared adapter patterns even when no specific prerequisite utility is required. | State `not applicable` explicitly if there is no stronger dependency. |
105	
106	## 17. Recommended Implementation Sequence
107	
108	1. Establish the machine/context boundary and confirm the documented part mapping.
109	2. Establish any required refs, provider context, or registration surfaces.
110	3. Derive attrs and render the documented structural nodes.
111	4. Wire prop sync and normalized event handling.
112	5. Add SSR/client-only guards, cleanup, and verify the documented test oracles.
113	
114	## 18. Anti-Patterns
115	
116	- Do not mirror machine-owned state in unsynchronized local adapter state.
117	- Do not bypass the documented attr merge order or replace required structural nodes with equivalent-looking wrappers.
118	- Do not re-register listeners, timers, observers, or registries on every render when the governing configuration has not changed.
119	
120	## 19. Consumer Expectations and Guarantees
121	
122	- Consumers may assume published `GroupContext` carries inherited disabled, invalid, and read-only state.
123	- Consumers may assume root `aria-disabled` is only part of the accessibility contract, not the descendant enforcement mechanism.
124	- Consumers must not assume root ARIA alone disables descendants without reading group context.
125	
126	## 20. Platform Support Matrix
127	
128	| Capability / behavior                     | Browser client | SSR          | Notes                                                                                         |
129	| ----------------------------------------- | -------------- | ------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
130	| documented structural and state semantics | full support   | full support | This utility does not have additional platform variance beyond its existing SSR/client rules. |
131	
132	## 21. Debug Diagnostics and Production Policy
133	
134	| Condition                                                            | Debug build behavior | Production behavior | Notes                                                                               |
135	| -------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------- | ------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
136	| no component-specific diagnostics beyond documented failure policies | not applicable       | not applicable      | Use the `Failure and Degradation Rules` section as the full runtime policy surface. |
137	
138	## 22. Shared Adapter Helper Notes
139	
140	| Helper concept    | Required?      | Responsibility                                                                          | Reused by      | Notes                                                         |
141	| ----------------- | -------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------- |
142	| attr merge helper | not applicable | No special helper beyond the documented attr derivation and merge contract is required. | not applicable | Use the normal machine attr derivation path for this utility. |
143	
144	## 23. Framework-Specific Behavior
145	
146	Leptos can render the root directly with spread attrs.
147	
148	## 24. Canonical Implementation Sketch
149	
150	```rust
151	#[component]
152	pub fn Group(children: Children) -> impl IntoView {
153	    let api = group::Api::new(group::Props::default());
154	    view! { <div {..api.root_attrs()}>{children()}</div> }
155	}
156	```
157	
158	## 25. Reference Implementation Skeleton
159	
160	No expanded skeleton beyond the canonical sketch is required for this utility.
161	
162	## 26. Adapter Invariants
163	
164	- Group semantics must remain structurally explicit even when the rendered node is visually neutral.
165	- Inherited state exposure rules must remain explicit wherever parent state is surfaced to descendants.
166	- Root-level `aria-disabled` must not be treated as sufficient descendant disabling; adapter-owned context propagation must carry disabled semantics into participating children.
167	
168	## 27. Accessibility and SSR Notes
169	
170	Must preserve group semantics and labeling attrs.
171	
172	## 28. Parity Summary and Intentional Deviations
173	
174	Parity summary: full core prop and root parity.
175	
176	Intentional deviations: none.
177	
178	## 29. Test Scenarios
179	
180	- root mapping
181	- role and invalid or disabled attrs
182	- disabled group state propagates to descendants through context rather than root ARIA alone
183	
184	## 30. Test Oracle Notes
185	
186	| Behavior                               | Preferred oracle type | Notes                                                                                                       |
187	| -------------------------------------- | --------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
188	| structural rendering and part presence | rendered structure    | Verify the documented part mapping rather than incidental wrapper details.                                  |
189	| accessibility and state attrs          | DOM attrs             | Assert the normalized attrs emitted by the adapter-owned node.                                              |
190	| descendant disabled propagation        | context registration  | Verify descendants observe propagated group disabled state instead of relying only on root `aria-disabled`. |
191	
192	## 31. Implementation Checklist
193	
194	- [ ] All documented parts and structural nodes are rendered with the correct ownership model.
195	- [ ] Attr merge precedence matches the documented contract.
196	- [ ] Prop sync and event normalization follow the documented machine paths.
197	- [ ] Descendant disabled, invalid, and read-only propagation is implemented through `GroupContext`, not only root ARIA attrs.
198	- [ ] Cleanup releases every resource owned by the component instance.
199	- [ ] SSR/client boundary behavior matches the documented structure and test oracles.
200	
```

**Phase 1 Drift Table**

| ID | Sev | Spec says | Impl does | Best outcome | Land where |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | Low | Manual `impl Default` on Props | `#[derive(Default)]` | Keep derive (matches Separator/Field) | spec §1.1 |
| D2 | Low | No builder chain shown | Provides `new()` + 6 setters | Keep builder (workspace convention) | spec §1.1 |
| D3 | Low | `GroupContext` derives only `Clone, Debug, PartialEq` | Adds `Default, Eq` | Keep extras (`Default` needed by child-side `unwrap_or_default()`; `Eq` is free) | spec §1.2 |
| D4 | **M-H** | `Api<'a> { props: &'a Props }` | `Api { props: Props }` (owned) | Owned (every other stateless component is owned; both adapter sketches already assume owned) | spec §1.2 |
| D5 | Low | `fn group_context` | `const fn group_context` | Keep `const` | spec §1.2 |
| D6 | Low | Role assigned inline in match arms | `role_str` extracted, single `.set` | Keep extracted form | spec §1.2 |
| D7 | Low | Sequential `attrs.set(...);` | Chained `.set().set()` | Keep chained | spec §1.2 |

  "old_string": "/// Context propagated to descendant components.\n/// Children read this to inherit disabled/invalid/read_only state.\n#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]\npub struct GroupContext {\n    /// Whether the group is disabled.\n    pub disabled: bool,\n    /// Whether the group is in an invalid state.\n    pub invalid: bool,\n    /// Whether the group is read-only.\n    pub read_only: bool,\n}\n\n/// The API for the `Group` component.\npub struct Api<'a> {\n    props: &'a Props,\n}\n\nimpl<'a> Api<'a> {\n    pub fn new(props: &'a Props) -> Self {\n        Self { props }\n    }\n\n    /// Returns the `GroupContext` to provide to descendants.\n    pub fn group_context(&self) -> GroupContext {\n        GroupContext {\n            disabled: self.props.disabled,\n            invalid: self.props.invalid,\n            read_only: self.props.read_only,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Root element attributes.\n    pub fn root_attrs(&self) -> AttrMap {\n        let mut attrs = AttrMap::new();\n        attrs.set(HtmlAttr::Id, &self.props.id);\n        let [(scope_attr, scope_val), (part_attr, part_val)] = Part::Root.data_attrs();\n        attrs.set(scope_attr, scope_val);\n        attrs.set(part_attr, part_val);\n\n        // Role\n        match self.props.role {\n            GroupRole::Group => attrs.set(HtmlAttr::Role, \"group\"),\n            GroupRole::Region => attrs.set(HtmlAttr::Role, \"region\"),\n            GroupRole::Presentation => attrs.set(HtmlAttr::Role, \"presentation\"),\n        }\n\n        // State attributes\n        if self.props.disabled {\n            attrs.set(HtmlAttr::Aria(AriaAttr::Disabled), \"true\");\n            attrs.set_bool(HtmlAttr::Data(\"ars-disabled\"), true);\n        }\n        if self.props.invalid {\n            attrs.set(HtmlAttr::Aria(AriaAttr::Invalid), \"true\");\n            attrs.set_bool(HtmlAttr::Data(\"ars-invalid\"), true);\n        }\n        if self.props.read_only {\n            attrs.set(HtmlAttr::Aria(AriaAttr::ReadOnly), \"true\");\n            attrs.set_bool(HtmlAttr::Data(\"ars-readonly\"), true);\n        }\n        if let Some(dir) = self.props.dir {\n            attrs.set(HtmlAttr::Dir, dir.as_html_attr());\n        }\n\n        attrs\n    }\n}\n\nimpl ConnectApi for Api<'_> {\n    type Part = Part;\n\n    fn part_attrs(&self, part: Part) -> AttrMap {\n        match part {\n            Part::Root => self.root_attrs(),\n        }\n    }\n}\n```",
  "new_string": "/// Context propagated to descendant components.\n/// Children read this to inherit disabled/invalid/read_only state.\n///\n/// `Default` is derived so child components can fall back to a zero-state\n/// context via `.unwrap_or_default()` when no parent `Group` is present.\n#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub struct GroupContext {\n    /// Whether the group is disabled.\n    pub disabled: bool,\n    /// Whether the group is in an invalid state.\n    pub invalid: bool,\n    /// Whether the group is read-only.\n    pub read_only: bool,\n}\n\n/// The API for the `Group` component. Owns its `Props` so adapters can\n/// construct it once per render without tracking a separate borrow.\n#[derive(Clone, Debug)]\npub struct Api {\n    props: Props,\n}\n\nimpl Api {\n    pub const fn new(props: Props) -> Self {\n        Self { props }\n    }\n\n    /// Returns the `GroupContext` to provide to descendants.\n    pub const fn group_context(&self) -> GroupContext {\n        GroupContext {\n            disabled: self.props.disabled,\n            invalid: self.props.invalid,\n            read_only: self.props.read_only,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Root element attributes.\n    pub fn root_attrs(&self) -> AttrMap {\n        let mut attrs = AttrMap::new();\n        let [(scope_attr, scope_val), (part_attr, part_val)] = Part::Root.data_attrs();\n        attrs\n            .set(HtmlAttr::Id, &self.props.id)\n            .set(scope_attr, scope_val)\n            .set(part_attr, part_val);\n\n        // Role\n        let role_str = match self.props.role {\n            GroupRole::Group => \"group\",\n            GroupRole::Region => \"region\",\n            GroupRole::Presentation => \"presentation\",\n        };\n        attrs.set(HtmlAttr::Role, role_str);\n\n        // State attributes — emitted unconditionally regardless of role\n        // (see §3.1 for the accessibility rationale).\n        if self.props.disabled {\n            attrs\n                .set(HtmlAttr::Aria(AriaAttr::Disabled), \"true\")\n                .set_bool(HtmlAttr::Data(\"ars-disabled\"), true);\n        }\n        if self.props.invalid {\n            attrs\n                .set(HtmlAttr::Aria(AriaAttr::Invalid), \"true\")\n                .set_bool(HtmlAttr::Data(\"ars-invalid\"), true);\n        }\n        if self.props.read_only {\n            attrs\n                .set(HtmlAttr::Aria(AriaAttr::ReadOnly), \"true\")\n                .set_bool(HtmlAttr::Data(\"ars-readonly\"), true);\n        }\n        if let Some(dir) = self.props.dir {\n            attrs.set(HtmlAttr::Dir, dir.as_html_attr());\n        }\n\n        attrs\n    }\n}\n\nimpl ConnectApi for Api {\n    type Part = Part;\n\n    fn part_attrs(&self, part: Part) -> AttrMap {\n        match part {\n            Part::Root => self.root_attrs(),\n        }\n    }\n}\n```"
}

1	//! Shared helpers for spec-conformance tests.
2	//!
3	//! Per-component tests call [`assert_anatomy`] with the expected scope and
4	//! the expected `(part, kebab-name)` list. The helper checks every facet
5	//! of the `ComponentPart` contract that the spec's §3 anatomy table
6	//! declares:
7	//!
8	//! 1. `Part::scope()` matches the spec's `data-ars-scope` token.
9	//! 2. `Part::all()` produces exactly the declared parts in the declared
10	//!    order.
11	//! 3. Every declared part round-trips through `Part::name()` with the
12	//!    declared kebab-case token.
13	//! 4. `Part::ROOT` equals the first declared part (workspace convention:
14	//!    every `Part` enum's first variant is `Root`).
15	
16	use ars_core::ComponentPart;
17	
18	/// Asserts that the supplied `Part` enum matches the spec's anatomy
19	/// declaration.
20	///
21	/// `expected_scope` is the `#[scope = "..."]` token the spec puts on the
22	/// `data-ars-scope` attribute. `expected_parts` is the ordered list of
23	/// `(part, kebab-name)` pairs from the spec's §3 anatomy table.
24	pub(crate) fn assert_anatomy<P>(expected_scope: &'static str, expected_parts: &[(P, &'static str)])
25	where
26	    P: ComponentPart + core::fmt::Debug + PartialEq,
27	{
28	    assert_eq!(
29	        P::scope(),
30	        expected_scope,
31	        "Part::scope() must match spec §3 anatomy",
32	    );
33	
34	    let actual = P::all();
35	
36	    let actual_names = actual.iter().map(ComponentPart::name).collect::<Vec<_>>();
37	
38	    let expected_names = expected_parts
39	        .iter()
40	        .map(|(_, name)| *name)
41	        .collect::<Vec<_>>();
42	
43	    assert_eq!(
44	        actual_names, expected_names,
45	        "Part::all() name list must equal spec §3 anatomy column \
46	         (scope = {expected_scope:?})",
47	    );
48	
49	    assert_eq!(
50	        actual.len(),
51	        expected_parts.len(),
52	        "Part::all() length must match the number of spec §3 anatomy rows",
53	    );
54	
55	    for (actual_part, (expected_part, expected_name)) in actual.iter().zip(expected_parts) {
56	        assert_eq!(
57	            actual_part.name(),
58	            *expected_name,
59	            "Part {actual_part:?} must produce kebab-name {expected_name:?}",
60	        );
61	        assert_eq!(
62	            actual_part, expected_part,
63	            "Part::all()[i] must equal the expected part value",
64	        );
65	    }
66	
67	    if let Some((first_expected, _)) = expected_parts.first() {
68	        assert_eq!(
69	            &P::ROOT,
70	            first_expected,
71	            "Workspace convention: Part::ROOT (the first variant) must \
72	             equal the first spec-declared part",
73	        );
74	    }
75	}
76	
```

# cargo-mutants 27 reads this file by default from the workspace root.
#
# Keep these filters limited to mutants that do not represent observable
# production behavior under the Nightly mutation profile. Test helper code is
# excluded because mutating the test harness itself does not prove library
# behavior. Debug formatting and non-debug warning shims are excluded because
# they are intentionally diagnostic-only and often generate equivalent mutants
# such as `Ok(Default::default())` or deleted no-op calls.

exclude_globs = [
  # `sorted_collection/collation.rs` is behind the non-default `i18n` feature,
  # so the default Nightly mutation profile cannot observe these mutants.
  'crates/ars-collections/src/sorted_collection/collation.rs'
]

exclude_re = [
  # Empty-vector spellings are equivalent (`Vec::new()` and `vec![]`).
  'with vec!\[\]',

  # Feature-gated code that is not compiled by the default Nightly mutation
  # profile cannot be observed by those tests.
  'format_(effect_names|follow_up_events|target_state|apply_description)',
  'Service<M>::new_hydrated',
  'impl serde::Serialize',
  'compose.rs:52:43: replace != with == in merge_attrs',
  'async_validator.rs:102:9: replace <impl AsyncValidator for AsyncFnValidator<F>>::validate_async',
  'async_validator.rs:142:9: replace AsyncFnValidator<F>::boxed',
  'built_in.rs:444:5: replace is_valid_email',
  'built_in.rs:446:.*is_valid_email',
  'built_in.rs:621:5: replace is_valid_url',
  'built_in.rs:622:.*is_valid_url',
  'built_in.rs:626:.*is_valid_url',
  'built_in.rs:633:.*is_valid_url',
  'validator.rs:262:9: delete match arm AriaAttr::DropEffect \| AriaAttr::Grabbed in supported_aria_attribute',
  'Key::uuid -> Self with Default::default\(\)',
  '<impl From<uuid::Uuid> for Key>::from -> Self with Default::default\(\)',
  'shared_state.rs:93:9: replace <impl Clone for SharedState<T>>::clone -> Self with Default::default\(\)',
  'z_index.rs:69:5: replace next_z_index_impl -> u32 with [01]',
  'z_index.rs:72:41: replace >= with < in next_z_index_impl',
  'z_index.rs:75:41: replace \+ with [-*] in next_z_index_impl',
```

```rust
use ars_i18n::Direction;

/// The ARIA role applied to the group container.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Default)]
pub enum GroupRole {
    /// `role="group"` — a set of related UI elements not important enough
    /// to be included in a page summary or table of contents.
    #[default]
    Group,
    /// `role="region"` — a landmark region that is significant enough to be
    /// listed in a page summary. Requires an accessible name.
    Region,
    /// `role="presentation"` — removes the grouping semantics. Children are
    /// still grouped visually but not semantically.
    Presentation,
}

/// Props for the `Group` component.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, PartialEq, HasId)]
pub struct Props {
    /// Component instance ID.
    pub id: String,
    /// Whether the group and all contained controls are disabled.
    /// Propagated to children via `GroupContext`.
    pub disabled: bool,
    /// Whether the group is in an invalid state.
    /// Propagated to children via `GroupContext`.
    pub invalid: bool,
```

# Task #205 — Group agnostic core — implementation summary

## Files changed

| File | Lines | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `crates/ars-components/src/utility/group.rs` | +694 (new) | Component module: `GroupRole`, `Props`, `GroupContext`, `Part`, `Api`, `ConnectApi`, 33 unit + 5 snapshot tests |
| `crates/ars-components/src/utility/mod.rs` | +4 | Registers `pub mod group` |
| `crates/ars-components/src/utility/snapshots/ars_components__utility__group__tests__*.snap` | +5 files | Reviewed snapshot fixtures (default, region, presentation, all_states, dir_rtl) |
| `crates/ars-components/tests/spec_conformance/utility.rs` | +13/-2 | `group_anatomy_matches_spec` |
| `spec/components/utility/group.md` | rewrite of §1.1, §1.2, §3.1 | Drift-fix: owned `Api`, builder chain mention, `GroupContext` derives `Default + Eq`, `group_context` is `const fn`, idiomatic chained `attrs.set(...).set(...)`, §3.1 prose rewritten to match impl semantics |

## Post-implementation-audit results

**Phase 1 — spec/impl drift (7 findings, all spec-only, all landed):**

| ID | Severity | Drift | Resolution |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | Low | `impl Default` written manually | spec → `#[derive(Default)]` |
| D2 | Low | Builder chain not shown in spec | spec mentions the chain after Props |
| D3 | Low | `GroupContext` missing `Default, Eq` | spec adds them (`Default` needed by `unwrap_or_default()` on the child side) |
| D4 | **M-H** | `Api<'a> { props: &'a Props }` | spec → owned `Api { props: Props }` (every other stateless component is owned; both adapter sketches already assumed owned) |
| D5 | Low | `group_context` non-const | spec → `const fn` |
| D6 | Low | Role inline-assigned in each match arm | spec → extracted `role_str` |
| D7 | Low | Sequential `attrs.set(...);` | spec → chained `.set(...).set(...)` |

Plus the §3.1 prose rewrite covered during planning (state attrs emit unconditionally; cites WAI-ARIA 1.2 §5.4 + React Aria parity).

**Phase 2 — iterative passes:**
- **Round 1:** 1 finding — missing spec-conformance anatomy test → added.
- **Round 2:** clean.
- **Round 3:** clean. Terminated.

**Phase 3 — test coverage:**
- 100% line / region / function coverage on `group.rs`.
- `cargo mutants`: **0 missed**, 5 caught, 7 unviable (the unviable ones are const-fn / builder-return-self where `Default::default()` can't compile in place — a free correctness signal).
- Test-type breadth: unit ✓ snapshot ✓ spec-conformance ✓ mutation ✓. Proptest and doctest skipped per workspace convention for stateless components.

## Final verification

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `cargo build -p ars-components --lib` | Clean |
| `cargo test -p ars-components --lib --all-features` | 1546 passed, 0 failed |
| `cargo test -p ars-components --test spec_conformance` | 4 passed, including new `group_anatomy_matches_spec` |
| `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features --exclude ars-i18n -- -D warnings` | 0 warnings |
| `cargo insta test --workspace -p ars-components --unreferenced=reject` | No unreferenced snapshots |
| `cargo xtask spec validate` | 340 files OK |
| `cargo test -p xtask --test spec_corpus_compile_snippets` | passed (spec edits compile) |
| `cargo llvm-cov` on group.rs | 100% / 100% / 100% |
| `cargo mutants -p ars-components -f .../group.rs` | 0 missed, 5 caught, 7 unviable |

## Out of scope (per plan)

- Leptos / Dioxus adapter components (separate tasks).
- Wiring `GroupContext` consumption into existing child components (cross-component follow-up tasks).

{
  "command": "git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nfeat(components): implement Group agnostic core (#205)\n\nAdd the framework-agnostic core of the `Group` utility component: a stateless\nsemantic grouping wrapper that publishes a `GroupContext` so descendant\ncomponents can inherit `disabled` / `invalid` / `read_only` state without\nre-implementing the propagation themselves.\n\nSpec changes (landed in the same PR per the spec-first policy):\n\n- §1.1: drop the manual `impl Default` (derive does the same thing) and note\n  the workspace-standard builder chain.\n- §1.2: drop the `Api<'a>` lifetime in favor of owned `Api { props: Props }`\n  matching every other stateless utility; add `Default` and `Eq` derives to\n  `GroupContext` so child components can use `unwrap_or_default()`; mark\n  `group_context` `const fn`; replace sequential `.set()` calls with the\n  idiomatic chained form; extract the role string before assignment.\n- §3.1: replace the \"state attributes are omitted when role=Presentation\"\n  prose (which contradicted both the §1.2 code example and React Aria parity)\n  with a note clarifying that state attributes are always emitted, citing\n  WAI-ARIA 1.2 §5.4 and the §5.1 parity commitment.\n\nTests:\n\n- 33 inline unit tests covering Props/Api accessors, role mapping, the\n  `group_context()` propagation surface, root state attributes (including the\n  presentation-role behavior), `dir` forwarding, and `ConnectApi` dispatch\n  across every output-affecting prop combination.\n- 5 snapshot fixtures (default, region, presentation, all-states, dir=rtl).\n- New `group_anatomy_matches_spec` entry in the utility spec-conformance test.\n\nCoverage: 100% line / region / function on `group.rs`. `cargo mutants` reports\n0 missed mutations (5 caught, 7 unviable from `const fn` / builder-return-self\nsubstitutions that fail to compile, which is the desired correctness signal).\n\nCloses #205\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -15",
  "description": "Commit Group implementation"
}

Codex reviews asynchronously — it leaves a 👀 reaction on the PR while reviewing, and a 👍 reaction when satisfied. This skill defines the polling loop and the response workflow: wait for Codex to react, address any review threads it posts with the same rigor as `post-implementation-audit` (TDD + no coverage regression), commit, push, reply inline, resolve the thread, then keep waiting until 👍. Triggers on phrases like \"wait for codex review\", \"what does codex say\", \"is codex done\", \"address the codex feedback\", \"check the PR reviews\", or any post-push handoff where the user expects an automated reviewer's verdict. Invoke this skill even when the user does not explicitly name Codex — any GitHub PR where a 👀/👍 reaction signals an automated reviewer follows the same pattern.\n---\n\n# Waiting for Codex Review\n\nCodex (the OpenAI Codex automated reviewer) reviews open PRs asynchronously. The contract is:\n\n- **Run starts** → Codex adds a 👀 (eyes) reaction to the PR.\n- **Approved** → Codex adds a 👍 (thumbsup) reaction to the PR. No further action required.\n- **Changes requested** → Codex posts one or more inline review threads. 👀 typically stays until the next run completes after your fix.\n\nThis skill is the polling + response loop for that contract. You stay in it until 👍 lands.\n\n## When to invoke\n\nInvoke as soon as a PR is pushed and Codex is set up for the repository. Common trigger phrases:\n\n- \"wait for the codex review\"\n- \"what does codex say about the PR\"\n- \"address the codex feedback\"\n- \"check the PR reviews\"\n- \"is codex still reviewing\"\n\nIf the user pushes a PR and gives no explicit next instruction, default to invoking this skill — the right next step is to watch for Codex.\n\n## Reaction lookups: the exact data\n\nPRs are issues for the reactions endpoint. The bot's login depends on the install — on installs that use the ChatGPT Codex connector it's `chatgpt-codex-connector`; on the standalone Codex Cloud install it may be `codex[bot]` or similar. The first poll of this loop **discovers the bot login** rather than hard-coding it: any review-comment author whose handle contains `codex` is treated as Codex.\n\n```bash\n# Reactions on the PR itself\ngh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/issues/<pr>/reactions\n# → array of { user.login, content } where content ∈ {'+1','-1','laugh','confused','heart','hooray','rocket','eyes'}\n# +1 == 👍, eyes == 👀\n\n# Reviews and review threads\ngh pr view <pr> --json reviews\ngh api graphql -f query='\n  query($owner:String!,$repo:String!,$n:Int!){\n    repository(owner:$owner,name:$repo){\n      pullRequest(number:$n){\n        reviewThreads(first:100){\n          nodes{\n            id            # GraphQL node id — use this to RESOLVE\n            isResolved\n            comments(first:100){\n              nodes{ databaseId author{login} path line body }\n              # databaseId is the REST id — use this to REPLY\n            }\n          }\n        }\n      }\n    }\n  }' -F owner=<owner> -F repo=<repo> -F n=<pr>\n```\n\nKeep two ids per thread straight: **`thread.id`** (GraphQL node id, opaque string starting with `PRRT_...`) is needed to *resolve* the thread; **`comment.databaseId`** (integer) is needed to *reply* in-line. They are not interchangeable.\n\n## The loop\n\n```dot\ndigraph codex_loop {\n    poll [label=\"Poll reactions + threads\", shape=diamond];\n    thumbs [label=\"Codex 👍 on PR?\", shape=diamond];\n    threads [label=\"Unresolved Codex threads?\", shape=diamond];\n    address [label=\"Address each thread (TDD)\", shape=box];\n    push [label=\"Commit + push fix\", shape=box];\n    reply [label=\"Reply inline + resolve thread\", shape=box];\n    wait [label=\"Sleep, then re-poll\", shape=box];\n    done [label=\"Done — hand back to user\", shape=doublecircle];\n\n    poll -> thumbs;\n    thumbs -> done [label=\"yes\"];\n    thumbs -> threads [label=\"no\"];\n    threads -> address [label=\"yes\"];\n    threads -> wait [label=\"no (👀 only)\"];\n    wait -> poll;\n    address -> push -> reply -> poll;\n}\n```\n\n## Step-by-step\n\n### 1. Identify the PR\n\nIf the user names the PR, use that number. Otherwise, fall back to the head branch:\n\n```bash\ngh pr view --json number,url,headRefName\n```\n\nConfirm the PR is open and was pushed in this session before entering the loop — don't enter the loop on a stale branch.\n\n### 2. Snapshot the current state\n\nBefore polling, capture the state so the next poll can detect *new* signals (not just any signals):\n\n```text\nseen_reactions := { (user, content) ... } from /issues/<pr>/reactions\nseen_threads   := { thread.id ... } from the GraphQL query\n```\n\n### 3. Poll, decide, act\n\n| Signal observed | Decision |\n|---|---|\n| Codex 👍 reaction present | Loop is done. Announce. |\n| 👀 only, no new threads since `seen_threads` | Sleep, then re-poll. |\n| Unresolved Codex threads (new or carried over) | Drop into the \"address comments\" subroutine. |\n\nDecision-making notes:\n\n- **Codex sometimes posts a top-level review body** (\"LGTM\" / a short summary) without 👍. Treat that as advisory — wait for the actual 👍 reaction.\n- **A new push retriggers Codex**: after you push a fix, 👀 should reappear; if it doesn't within ~3 minutes, double-check that the push actually landed on the PR branch (`gh pr view <pr> --json commits`).\n- **Multiple Codex passes are normal.** The loop must tolerate Codex finding new issues introduced by your fix.\n\n### 4. Address review comments (the hard part)\n\nEach unresolved Codex thread is a finding that must land a fix in *this* PR — no follow-up issues, no deferral. The bar is the same as the `post-implementation-audit` skill (read it if you haven't):\n\n1. **Read the comment twice** before you start. Then read the surrounding 30 lines of the file. Codex's comments are usually right but sometimes context-thin — your job is to understand whether the suggested change is the *best outcome* or whether the underlying concern can be fixed differently. If you disagree, see \"Disagreeing with Codex\" below.\n2. **Write the test first.** For every code change you make, add or update a named test that *fails on the current code* and *passes after the fix*. Skipping the test is what lets the regression come back silently on the next iteration. This applies even to \"obvious\" fixes — obvious code regresses too.\n3. **Coverage must not regress.** Capture the current per-line / per-region / per-branch numbers on the touched module *before* changing anything. After the fix, re-run the same command. If any number drops, find the missing test and add it before committing.\n4. **Workspace conventions still apply.** Lint flags, derive lists, `no_std` constraints, doc comments on public items, spec-as-contract — all unchanged. Codex's suggestion is a finding, not a license to skip the project rules.\n5. **Commit with a thread-linked message.** The commit message names the issue Codex raised, not just the file you touched. Example: `fix(group): clamp role on legacy adapters (Codex review #648)`.\n\n### 5. Push, reply inline, resolve\n\nAfter pushing:\n\n**Reply** on each thread you addressed. Use the first comment's `databaseId` as the parent:\n\n```bash\ngh api -X POST repos/<owner>/<repo>/pulls/<pr>/comments \\\n  -F in_reply_to=<parent_databaseId> \\\n  -F body=$'Fixed in <commit-sha>: <one-line summary of the fix>'\n```\n\n**Resolve** the thread via GraphQL (uses the node id, not the database id):\n\n```bash\ngh api graphql -f query='\n  mutation($threadId:ID!){\n    resolveReviewThread(input:{threadId:$threadId}){ thread { isResolved } }\n  }' -F threadId=<thread.id>\n```\n\nOnly resolve threads where you actually landed a fix or replied with a justification the user has approved. Resolving without a real fix breaks the next reviewer's trust in the resolved-status signal.\n\n### 6. Loop\n\nReturn to step 2 with refreshed `seen_*` sets. Codex's next pass either approves (👍) or surfaces new findings.\n\n## Polling cadence\n\nCodex review runs typically complete in 1–5 minutes for a small PR, 10–20 for a large one. Pick the cadence by what you're waiting for:\n\n- **First poll after push**: 60 seconds.\n- **Routine poll while 👀 is up**: 120 seconds (stays inside Anthropic's 5-min prompt cache window).\n- **No reaction at all after 5 minutes**: ping the user — Codex may not be configured for this repo, or the install is stalled.\n- **👀 has been up >15 minutes with no comment activity**: ping the user.\n\nFor dynamic-paced `/loop` runs, use `ScheduleWakeup` with `delaySeconds=120` (cache-warm) until 👍 lands. Don't poll faster than 60 seconds — Codex doesn't react that quickly anyway, and the prompt cache cost dominates.\n\nFor event-driven monitoring (one notification per state change instead of fixed-interval polling), `Monitor` works well:\n\n```bash\nprev=\"\"\nwhile true; do\n  reactions=$(gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/issues/<pr>/reactions \\\n    --jq '.[] | \"\\(.user.login):\\(.content)\"' 2>/dev/null | sort)\n  threads=$(gh api graphql -f query='query($o:String!,$r:String!,$n:Int!){\n    repository(owner:$o,name:$r){pullRequest(number:$n){\n      reviewThreads(first:100){nodes{id isResolved comments(first:1){nodes{author{login}}}}}}}}' \\\n    -F o=<owner> -F r=<repo> -F n=<pr> \\\n    --jq '.data.repository.pullRequest.reviewThreads.nodes\n          | map(select(.isResolved==false\n                       and (.comments.nodes[0].author.login | test(\"codex\"; \"i\"))))\n          | length' 2>/dev/null)\n  cur=\"reactions=$reactions threads=$threads\"\n  [ \"$cur\" != \"$prev\" ] && echo \"[change] $cur\"\n  prev=$cur\n  echo \"$reactions\" | grep -q \"codex.*+1$\" && { echo \"[approval]\"; break; }\n  sleep 120\ndone\n```\n\n## Disagreeing with Codex\n\nCodex is usually right but not always. When you read a thread and your read of the spec or code says Codex is wrong (or redundant, or stylistic-without-justification):\n\n1. **Do not unilaterally dismiss it.** Stop and surface to the user with: what Codex says, what the spec / code says, your reasoning, and a recommendation (address as-is / push back / close-as-wontfix).\n2. **The user picks.** Then follow through — if \"push back\", reply inline with the technical reasoning; if \"close-as-wontfix\", reply inline with the rationale before resolving; if \"address\", fall through to step 4 of the loop.\n3. **Never reply inline arguing the point without checking with the user first.** Codex's threads are read by humans too — a wrong reply costs more than a one-minute clarification.\n\n## Anti-patterns\n\n- **Resolving threads without a real fix or explicit user disagreement.** Marking resolved is load-bearing — the next reviewer trusts it. An unaddressed finding hidden by a resolution is invisible until production.\n- **Skipping the test** because \"the fix is obvious.\" Obvious fixes regress just as often as subtle ones. The test exists to prove the fix held over time, not to prove the fix was needed today.\n- **Replying with \"fixed\" only.** Quote the commit sha and a one-line summary so the thread is self-contained when read later.\n- **Polling faster than 60 seconds.** Burns prompt cache for no benefit. Codex doesn't react that fast.\n- **Continuing past a 👍 in case there are still threads.** 👍 means Codex is satisfied with the current state — pending threads are stale. Re-verify with the user before bulk-resolving.\n- **Arguing with Codex inline without escalating.** A wrong reply is more expensive than a one-minute user clarification.\n- **Hard-coding the bot login.** Different installs use different handles (`chatgpt-codex-connector`, `codex[bot]`, etc.). Match by case-insensitive substring instead.\n\n## What this skill is not\n\n- **A code-review skill.** Actual fixes come from project-specific implementation skills (`post-implementation-audit`, `test-driven-development`, `systematic-debugging`). This skill is the wrapper that decides *when* those skills fire and how the result is reported back to GitHub.\n- **A merge skill.** 👍 doesn't auto-merge — the user owns the merge decision. Stop the loop on 👍, hand back, let them merge.\n- **A way to delegate judgment back to Codex.** If you disagree with a finding, surface to the user. Don't argue with the bot.\n"
}
```

Skill drafted at `/Users/ericson/.claude/skills/waiting-for-codex-review/SKILL.md` (user-level so it's reusable across projects). Highlights:

- **Trigger description** pulls in the realistic phrases ("wait for codex review", "what does codex say", "is codex done", "address the codex feedback", etc.) and tells the model to invoke even when Codex isn't explicitly named.
- **Reaction contract** spelled out (👀 = reviewing, 👍 = approved, otherwise threads + carry-over 👀).

- **Two ids per thread, kept straight:** `thread.id` (GraphQL node id) for resolve, `comment.databaseId` (REST integer) for reply. Common landmine, called out explicitly.
- **The hard part — addressing findings** — points at `post-implementation-audit` for the rigor bar and lays out the TDD-first + no-coverage-regression contract verbatim.
- **Reply + resolve workflow** with the exact `gh api` invocations.
- **Disagreement protocol**: escalate to user with the spec-vs-Codex framing before replying inline. No unilateral dismissals.
- **Polling cadence**: 60 s first poll, 120 s steady-state (cache-warm), >5 min no-reaction → ping user, >15 min 👀 with no activity → ping user.
- **Monitor snippet** for event-driven polling (one notification per state change) so the loop doesn't drown the conversation.

6	# Waiting for Codex Review
7	
8	Codex (the OpenAI Codex automated reviewer) reviews open PRs asynchronously. The contract is:

10	- **Run starts** → Codex adds a 👀 (eyes) reaction to the PR.

19	
20	- "wait for the codex review"
21	- "what does codex say about the PR"
22	- "address the codex feedback"
23	- "check the PR reviews"
24	- "is codex still reviewing"
25	
26	If the user pushes a PR and gives no explicit next instruction, default to invoking this skill — the right next step is to watch for Codex.
27	
28	## Reaction lookups: the exact data
29	
30	PRs are issues for the reactions endpoint. The bot's login depends on the install — on installs that use the ChatGPT Codex connector it's `chatgpt-codex-connector`; on the standalone Codex Cloud install it may be `codex[bot]` or similar. The first poll of this loop **discovers the bot login** rather than hard-coding it: any review-comment author whose handle contains `codex` is treated as Codex.
31	
32	```bash
33	# Reactions on the PR itself
34	gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/issues/<pr>/reactions
35	# → array of { user.login, content } where content ∈ {'+1','-1','laugh','confused','heart','hooray','rocket','eyes'}
36	# +1 == 👍, eyes == 👀
37	
38	# Reviews and review threads
39	gh pr view <pr> --json reviews
40	gh api graphql -f query='
41	  query($owner:String!,$repo:String!,$n:Int!){
42	    repository(owner:$owner,name:$repo){
43	      pullRequest(number:$n){
44	        reviewThreads(first:100){
45	          nodes{
46	            id            # GraphQL node id — use this to RESOLVE
47	            isResolved
48	            comments(first:100){
49	              nodes{ databaseId author{login} path line body }
50	              # databaseId is the REST id — use this to REPLY
51	            }
52	          }
53	        }
54	      }
55	    }
56	  }' -F owner=<owner> -F repo=<repo> -F n=<pr>
57	```
58	
59	Keep two ids per thread straight: **`thread.id`** (GraphQL node id, opaque string starting with `PRRT_...`) is needed to *resolve* the thread; **`comment.databaseId`** (integer) is needed to *reply* in-line. They are not interchangeable.
60	
61	## The loop
62	
63	```dot
64	digraph codex_loop {
65	    poll [label="Poll reactions + threads", shape=diamond];
66	    thumbs [label="Codex 👍 on PR?", shape=diamond];
67	    threads [label="Unresolved Codex threads?", shape=diamond];
68	    address [label="Address each thread (TDD)", shape=box];
69	    push [label="Commit + push fix", shape=box];
70	    reply [label="Reply inline + resolve thread", shape=box];
71	    wait [label="Sleep, then re-poll", shape=box];
72	    done [label="Done — hand back to user", shape=doublecircle];
73	
74	    poll -> thumbs;
75	    thumbs -> done [label="yes"];
76	    thumbs -> threads [label="no"];
77	    threads -> address [label="yes"];
78	    threads -> wait [label="no (👀 only)"];
79	    wait -> poll;
80	    address -> push -> reply -> poll;
81	}
82	```
83	
84	## Step-by-step
85	
86	### 1. Identify the PR
87	
88	If the user names the PR, use that number. Otherwise, fall back to the head branch:
89	
90	```bash
```

Codex reviews asynchronously — it leaves a 👀 reaction on the PR while reviewing, drops the 👀 when the run finishes (either replacing it with 👍 if satisfied, or removing it and posting review threads if changes are needed). This skill defines the polling loop and the response workflow: wait for Codex's verdict, address any review threads it posts with the same rigor as `post-implementation-audit` (TDD + no coverage regression), commit, push, reply inline, resolve the thread, then keep waiting until 👍. Triggers on phrases like \"wait for codex review\", \"what does codex say\", \"is codex done\", \"address the codex feedback\", \"check the PR reviews\", or any post-push handoff where the user expects an automated reviewer's verdict. Invoke this skill even when the user does not explicitly name Codex — any GitHub PR where a 👀/👍 reaction signals an automated reviewer follows the same pattern.\n---\n\n# Waiting for Codex Review\n\nCodex (the OpenAI Codex automated reviewer) reviews open PRs asynchronously. The reaction contract on the PR itself:\n\n- **Run starts** → Codex adds 👀 (eyes). This is the \"I'm looking\" signal.\n- **Run finishes — approved** → Codex *removes* 👀 and adds 👍 (thumbsup). No further action required.\n- **Run finishes — changes requested** → Codex *removes* 👀 and posts one or more inline review threads. **No 👍 is added.** The disappearance of 👀 is the signal that this run finished with findings.\n- **You push a fix** → Codex re-adds 👀 to start the next pass. The loop resumes.\n\nThe **👀-disappears** transition is load-bearing: it's how you distinguish \"still reviewing\" from \"review done — go look at the threads\". Don't keep waiting for 👍 once 👀 has dropped; switch to the thread-handling subroutine immediately.\n\nThis skill is the polling + response loop for that contract. You stay in it until 👍 lands."

  "new_string": "## The loop\n\n```dot\ndigraph codex_loop {\n    poll [label=\"Poll reactions + threads\", shape=diamond];\n    thumbs [label=\"Codex 👍 on PR?\", shape=diamond];\n    eyes [label=\"Codex 👀 on PR?\", shape=diamond];\n    threads [label=\"Unresolved Codex threads?\", shape=diamond];\n    address [label=\"Address each thread (TDD)\", shape=box];\n    push [label=\"Commit + push fix\", shape=box];\n    reply [label=\"Reply inline + resolve thread\", shape=box];\n    wait_review [label=\"Sleep — Codex still reviewing\", shape=box];\n    wait_start [label=\"Sleep — Codex hasn't started\", shape=box];\n    done [label=\"Done — hand back to user\", shape=doublecircle];\n\n    poll -> thumbs;\n    thumbs -> done [label=\"yes\"];\n    thumbs -> eyes [label=\"no\"];\n    eyes -> wait_review [label=\"yes (still reviewing)\"];\n    eyes -> threads [label=\"no (👀 dropped → run finished)\"];\n    threads -> address [label=\"yes\"];\n    threads -> wait_start [label=\"no (pre-review, no run yet)\"];\n    wait_review -> poll;\n    wait_start -> poll;\n    address -> push -> reply -> poll;\n}\n```\n\n**Three terminal poll states matter:**\n\n| State | What it means | What to do |\n|---|---|---|\n| 👍 present (👀 absent) | Codex approved | Done — hand back to user |\n| 👀 present | Codex is actively reviewing right now | Sleep, re-poll |\n| 👀 absent AND 👍 absent | Either Codex finished with findings, or it never started | Check threads — if any unresolved Codex thread exists, address them; otherwise treat as \"not started yet\" and sleep |"

100	## Step-by-step
101	
102	### 1. Identify the PR
103	
104	If the user names the PR, use that number. Otherwise, fall back to the head branch:
105	
106	```bash
107	gh pr view --json number,url,headRefName
108	```
109	
110	Confirm the PR is open and was pushed in this session before entering the loop — don't enter the loop on a stale branch.
111	
112	### 2. Snapshot the current state
113	
114	Before polling, capture the state so the next poll can detect *new* signals (not just any signals):
115	
116	```text
117	seen_reactions := { (user, content) ... } from /issues/<pr>/reactions
118	seen_threads   := { thread.id ... } from the GraphQL query
119	```
120	
121	### 3. Poll, decide, act
122	
123	| Signal observed | Decision |
124	|---|---|
125	| Codex 👍 reaction present | Loop is done. Announce. |
126	| 👀 only, no new threads since `seen_threads` | Sleep, then re-poll. |
127	| Unresolved Codex threads (new or carried over) | Drop into the "address comments" subroutine. |
128	
129	Decision-making notes:
130	
131	- **Codex sometimes posts a top-level review body** ("LGTM" / a short summary) without 👍. Treat that as advisory — wait for the actual 👍 reaction.
132	- **A new push retriggers Codex**: after you push a fix, 👀 should reappear; if it doesn't within ~3 minutes, double-check that the push actually landed on the PR branch (`gh pr view <pr> --json commits`).
133	- **Multiple Codex passes are normal.** The loop must tolerate Codex finding new issues introduced by your fix.
134	
135	### 4. Address review comments (the hard part)
136	
137	Each unresolved Codex thread is a finding that must land a fix in *this* PR — no follow-up issues, no deferral. The bar is the same as the `post-implementation-audit` skill (read it if you haven't):
138	
139	1. **Read the comment twice** before you start. Then read the surrounding 30 lines of the file. Codex's comments are usually right but sometimes context-thin — your job is to understand whether the suggested change is the *best outcome* or whether the underlying concern can be fixed differently. If you disagree, see "Disagreeing with Codex" below.
140	2. **Write the test first.** For every code change you make, add or update a named test that *fails on the current code* and *passes after the fix*. Skipping the test is what lets the regression come back silently on the next iteration. This applies even to "obvious" fixes — obvious code regresses too.
141	3. **Coverage must not regress.** Capture the current per-line / per-region / per-branch numbers on the touched module *before* changing anything. After the fix, re-run the same command. If any number drops, find the missing test and add it before committing.
142	4. **Workspace conventions still apply.** Lint flags, derive lists, `no_std` constraints, doc comments on public items, spec-as-contract — all unchanged. Codex's suggestion is a finding, not a license to skip the project rules.
143	5. **Commit with a thread-linked message.** The commit message names the issue Codex raised, not just the file you touched. Example: `fix(group): clamp role on legacy adapters (Codex review #648)`.
144	
145	### 5. Push, reply inline, resolve
146	
147	After pushing:
148	
149	**Reply** on each thread you addressed. Use the first comment's `databaseId` as the parent:
150	
151	```bash
152	gh api -X POST repos/<owner>/<repo>/pulls/<pr>/comments \
153	  -F in_reply_to=<parent_databaseId> \
154	  -F body=$'Fixed in <commit-sha>: <one-line summary of the fix>'
155	```
156	
157	**Resolve** the thread via GraphQL (uses the node id, not the database id):
158	
159	```bash
160	gh api graphql -f query='
161	  mutation($threadId:ID!){
162	    resolveReviewThread(input:{threadId:$threadId}){ thread { isResolved } }
163	  }' -F threadId=<thread.id>
164	```
165	
166	Only resolve threads where you actually landed a fix or replied with a justification the user has approved. Resolving without a real fix breaks the next reviewer's trust in the resolved-status signal.
167	
168	### 6. Loop
169	
170	Return to step 2 with refreshed `seen_*` sets. Codex's next pass either approves (👍) or surfaces new findings.
171	
172	## Polling cadence
173	
174	Codex review runs typically complete in 1–5 minutes for a small PR, 10–20 for a large one. Pick the cadence by what you're waiting for:
175	
176	- **First poll after push**: 60 seconds.
177	- **Routine poll while 👀 is up**: 120 seconds (stays inside Anthropic's 5-min prompt cache window).
178	- **No reaction at all after 5 minutes**: ping the user — Codex may not be configured for this repo, or the install is stalled.
179	- **👀 has been up >15 minutes with no comment activity**: ping the user.
180	
181	For dynamic-paced `/loop` runs, use `ScheduleWakeup` with `delaySeconds=120` (cache-warm) until 👍 lands. Don't poll faster than 60 seconds — Codex doesn't react that quickly anyway, and the prompt cache cost dominates.
182	
183	For event-driven monitoring (one notification per state change instead of fixed-interval polling), `Monitor` works well:
184	
185	```bash
186	prev=""
187	while true; do
188	  reactions=$(gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/issues/<pr>/reactions \
189	    --jq '.[] | "\(.user.login):\(.content)"' 2>/dev/null | sort)
190	  threads=$(gh api graphql -f query='query($o:String!,$r:String!,$n:Int!){
191	    repository(owner:$o,name:$r){pullRequest(number:$n){
192	      reviewThreads(first:100){nodes{id isResolved comments(first:1){nodes{author{login}}}}}}}}' \
193	    -F o=<owner> -F r=<repo> -F n=<pr> \
194	    --jq '.data.repository.pullRequest.reviewThreads.nodes
195	          | map(select(.isResolved==false
196	                       and (.comments.nodes[0].author.login | test("codex"; "i"))))
197	          | length' 2>/dev/null)
198	  cur="reactions=$reactions threads=$threads"
199	  [ "$cur" != "$prev" ] && echo "[change] $cur"
200	  prev=$cur
201	  echo "$reactions" | grep -q "codex.*+1$" && { echo "[approval]"; break; }
202	  sleep 120
203	done
204	```
205	
206	## Disagreeing with Codex
207	
208	Codex is usually right but not always. When you read a thread and your read of the spec or code says Codex is wrong (or redundant, or stylistic-without-justification):
209	
210	1. **Do not unilaterally dismiss it.** Stop and surface to the user with: what Codex says, what the spec / code says, your reasoning, and a recommendation (address as-is / push back / close-as-wontfix).
211	2. **The user picks.** Then follow through — if "push back", reply inline with the technical reasoning; if "close-as-wontfix", reply inline with the rationale before resolving; if "address", fall through to step 4 of the loop.
212	3. **Never reply inline arguing the point without checking with the user first.** Codex's threads are read by humans too — a wrong reply costs more than a one-minute clarification.
213	
214	## Anti-patterns
215	
216	- **Resolving threads without a real fix or explicit user disagreement.** Marking resolved is load-bearing — the next reviewer trusts it. An unaddressed finding hidden by a resolution is invisible until production.
217	- **Skipping the test** because "the fix is obvious." Obvious fixes regress just as often as subtle ones. The test exists to prove the fix held over time, not to prove the fix was needed today.
218	- **Replying with "fixed" only.** Quote the commit sha and a one-line summary so the thread is self-contained when read later.
219	- **Polling faster than 60 seconds.** Burns prompt cache for no benefit. Codex doesn't react that fast.
```

Treat that as advisory — wait for the actual 👍 reaction.\n- **A new push retriggers Codex**: after you push a fix, 👀 should reappear; if it doesn't within ~3 minutes, double-check that the push actually landed on the PR branch (`gh pr view <pr> --json commits`).\n- **Multiple Codex passes are normal.** The loop must tolerate Codex finding new issues introduced by your fix.",

Each pass is a fresh 👀 → (👍 | ∅ + threads) cycle."

  "new_string": "## Polling cadence\n\nCodex review runs typically complete in 1–5 minutes for a small PR, 10–20 for a large one. Pick the cadence by what you're waiting for:\n\n- **First poll after push**: 60 seconds (Codex usually adds 👀 within 30–90 s).\n- **Routine poll while 👀 is up**: 120 seconds (stays inside Anthropic's 5-min prompt cache window).\n- **Routine poll while neither 👀 nor 👍 is present and no threads exist**: 120 seconds — Codex hasn't started yet (or finished a previous pass and a new one is pending).\n- **👀 dropped + threads exist**: don't poll — switch immediately to the thread-handling subroutine.\n- **No reaction at all after 5 minutes from push**: ping the user — Codex may not be configured for this repo, or the install is stalled. > AGENT

Skill updated. Key corrections:

1. **Reaction lifecycle clarified at the top**: 👀 is dropped when Codex finishes — either *replaced by 👍* (approved) or *removed alongside posted threads* (changes requested). 👍 is never added when changes are requested.
2. **Decision table is now a 4-state matrix** keyed on (👀 present, 👍 present, threads exist) — covers all three terminal poll states (approved, still-reviewing, finished-with-findings) plus the pre-review idle case.
3. **The 👀 → ∅ transition** is called out explicitly as the load-bearing signal so the model doesn't sit waiting for a 👍 that will never come.
4. **Monitor snippet** now emits `[needs_action]` when 👀 drops AND threads exist (in addition to the existing `[approval]` on 👍), so the polling loop exits on either terminal condition without burning more cache.
5. **Polling cadence** got a new row: "👀 dropped + threads exist → don't poll, switch to thread-handling immediately."

Take a look. After your next sign-off I'll commit the skill alongside the review fix, or as a separate commit if you prefer.

Use the first comment's `databaseId` as the parent:\n\n```bash\ngh api -X POST repos/<owner>/<repo>/pulls/<pr>/comments \\\n  -F in_reply_to=<parent_databaseId> \\\n  -F body=$'Fixed in <commit-sha>: <one-line summary of the fix>'\n```\n\n**Resolve** the thread via GraphQL (uses the node id, not the database id):\n\n```bash\ngh api graphql -f query='\n  mutation($threadId:ID!){\n    resolveReviewThread(input:{threadId:$threadId}){ thread { isResolved } }\n  }' -F threadId=<thread.id>\n```\n\nOnly resolve threads where you actually landed a fix or replied with a justification the user has approved. Codex's next pass either approves (👍) or surfaces new findings.",
  "new_string": "### 5. Push, reply inline, resolve\n\nAfter pushing:\n\n**Reply** on each thread you addressed. Use the first comment's `databaseId` as the parent:\n\n```bash\ngh api -X POST repos/<owner>/<repo>/pulls/<pr>/comments \\\n  -F in_reply_to=<parent_databaseId> \\\n  -F body=$'Fixed in <commit-sha>: <one-line summary of the fix>'\n```\n\n**Resolve** the thread via GraphQL (uses the node id, not the database id):\n\n```bash\ngh api graphql -f query='\n  mutation($threadId:ID!){\n    resolveReviewThread(input:{threadId:$threadId}){ thread { isResolved } }\n  }' -F threadId=<thread.id>\n```\n\nOnly resolve threads where you actually landed a fix or replied with a justification the user has approved. Resolving without a real fix breaks the next reviewer's trust in the resolved-status signal.\n\n### 6. Re-trigger Codex with `@codex review`\n\nAfter every addressed thread has been replied to and resolved, post a fresh top-level comment on the PR with the literal body `@codex review`. This is required because Codex does **not** reliably re-trigger from a new push alone — sometimes the post-push hook fires, sometimes it doesn't, and waiting silently is indistinguishable from \"Codex stalled\". The explicit mention is the deterministic way to start the next pass.\n\n```bash\ngh pr comment <pr> --body \"@codex review\"\n```\n\nOnly post this comment **after** the fix push and the thread replies/resolves have all landed — otherwise Codex starts the next pass against stale state and burns a full review cycle on outdated code.\n\nIf there's nothing to address but the loop is still waiting (e.g., 👀 was never added after a push), posting `@codex review` is also the right escalation before pinging the user.\n\n### 7. Loop\n\nReturn to step 2 with refreshed `seen_*` sets. Codex's next pass picks up the comment within ~30–90 s, re-adds 👀, and either approves (👍) or surfaces new findings."
}
```

Otherwise, fall back to the head branch:\n\n```bash\ngh pr view --json number,url,headRefName\n```\n\nConfirm the PR is open and was pushed in this session before entering the loop — don't enter the loop on a stale branch.\n\n### 2.   "new_string": "## Step-by-step\n\n### 1. Identify the PR\n\nIf the user names the PR, use that number. Otherwise, fall back to the head branch:\n\n```bash\ngh pr view --json number,url,headRefName\n```\n\nConfirm the PR is open and was pushed in this session before entering the loop — don't enter the loop on a stale branch.\n\n### 2. Kick off the initial review with `@codex review`\n\nCodex does not always start a review automatically when a PR is opened. Some installs only trigger on explicit mention, others trigger automatically *most* of the time but not always. Either way, posting `@codex review` as a top-level comment right after the first push is the deterministic way to start the first review — and it's an idempotent no-op when Codex would have triggered on its own (Codex just sees the redundant mention and starts).\n\n```bash\ngh pr comment <pr> --body \"@codex review\"\n```\n\nPost this **once** immediately after entering the loop on a freshly-pushed PR, **before** the first snapshot/poll. This is the same trigger used in step 6 for subsequent passes — the difference is just timing (entry vs. after a fix push). If you entered the loop on a PR that already has a 👀 or 👍 reaction from Codex (e.g., the user invoked the skill mid-stream), skip this step — the review is already in flight.\n\n### 3. Snapshot the current state"
}
