40	- Follow TDD: tests first, implementation second, verification last.
41	- Keep task scope aligned with the issue. If the issue is too large or ambiguous, stop and split or clarify rather than freelancing a bigger change.
42	- Prefer tasks sized `1`, `2`, `3`, or `5` points. `8` is exceptional. `13` must be decomposed before implementation.
43	- Preserve the crate and dependency layering defined by the architecture and implementation plan.
44	- Do not add a new dependency crate without explicit user approval first. If a task appears to need a new crate, stop, explain why, and get approval before editing any `Cargo.toml`.
45	- When a task is complete, verify the exact tests and checks named by the issue before considering it done.
46	
47	### Code Quality Standards
48	
49	- **Document all public items.** Every public struct, enum, trait, function, constant, type alias, method, field, and variant must have a `///` doc comment. Every `lib.rs` must have a `//!` crate-level doc comment. The workspace enforces `missing_docs` linting — undocumented public items are build failures.
50	- **Zero warnings policy.** All code must compile with zero warnings under the workspace's configured clippy and rustc lints. Fix the root cause instead of suppressing. When suppression is genuinely needed, use `#[expect(lint, reason = "...")]` — never `#[allow(...)]`.
51	- **Derive documentation from the spec.** Doc comments should describe the _purpose and semantics_ of the item as defined in the corresponding `spec/` files, not just restate the type signature.
52	- **Use `#[inline]` selectively, not mechanically.** Do **not** add Clippy's `missing_inline_in_public_items` lint at the workspace level, and do not treat public visibility alone as a reason to mark an item `#[inline]`. Use `#[inline]` for thin cross-crate wrappers, trivial accessors, and hot-path no-op shims where the call overhead is plausibly meaningful. Avoid blanket `#[inline]` on all public APIs — it increases code size, adds compile-time cost, and turns `#[inline]` into noise instead of a deliberate performance signal.
53	- **Use directory-backed modules with `mod.rs` when a module owns children.** This repo standardizes on the older filesystem layout for nested modules. If module `foo` has child modules, the parent must live at `foo/mod.rs`, not `foo.rs`. Do not mix `foo.rs` with a sibling `foo/` directory. When a refactor adds child modules under an existing flat file, move the parent into `foo/mod.rs` in the same change. Do not leave empty leftover module directories behind.
54	
55	### API Design Standards
56	
57	- **Design for the pit of success.** Public APIs should make the most natural, shortest path also be the path that is correct, accessible, localizable, type-safe, and performant. Prefer ergonomic constructors and defaults that preserve the spec's strongest guarantees instead of making users opt into correctness after choosing a convenient API.
58	- **Name escape hatches explicitly.** When a component needs a lower-level, static, non-i18n, or otherwise less complete path, keep it available but make the tradeoff visible in the API name or type. The blessed path should remain the simplest call site, while escape hatches should read as deliberate choices.
59	- **Keep semantic data separate from rendered views.** Rich framework views are useful for customization, but components still need semantic sources for accessible names, localized text, announcements, ids, and state-machine wiring. Do not rely on arbitrary rendered view trees as the only source of user-facing semantics.
60	
61	### Code Coverage
62	
63	The workspace uses `cargo-llvm-cov` for code coverage with per-crate threshold enforcement. CI runs coverage on every PR (see `.github/workflows/ci.yml`).
64

Based on my comprehensive exploration of `/Users/ericson/.t3/worktrees/ars-ui/t3code-e16f9343/xtask/src`, here is your 20-step CI analysis:

## Compact Summary Table

| # | Step | What it does | Cost | Value 1-5 | Skip Y/N? | Reason |
|---|------|-------------|------|-----------|-----------|--------|
| 1 | fmt | leptosfmt + dx fmt + cargo +nightly fmt --check | Medium (15-20s) | 5 | N | Catches syntax/formatting bugs; required before push |
| 2 | check | cargo check --all-features (ars-i18n split) | Medium (20-30s) | 5 | N | Type errors caught before unit tests; foundational |
| 3 | clippy | cargo clippy -D warnings (ars-i18n split) | Medium (25-35s) | 5 | N | Catches semantic/style warnings; must pass for merge |
| 4 | unit | cargo nextest run (all-targets, ars-i18n split) | Medium (20-40s) | 5 | N | All unit tests; baseline correctness |
| 5 | i18n-browser | wasm-pack test (ars-i18n web-intl backend) | Slow (45-60s) | 3 | Y | WASM-only, web-intl-specific backend; skip if no i18n changes |
| 6 | dom-browser | wasm-pack test (ars-dom web feature) | Slow (45-60s) | 3 | Y | WASM-only, DOM-specific; skip if no DOM/web changes |
| 7 | release | cargo check --release (ars-i18n split) + release tests | Slow (50-70s) | 4 | Y | Catches release-only compile failures; medium value but slowish |
| 8 | integration | cargo nextest run --lib (integration test suite) | Medium (20-40s) | 4 | N | Black-box E2E tests; catches integration bugs |
| 9 | adapter | cargo nextest run (leptos + dioxus harness tests) | Medium (30-50s) | 5 | N | Framework adapter tests; must verify both adapters work |
| 10 | adapter-parity | Python lint: test count delta between adapters ≤2 | Fast (<5s) | 3 | Y | Lint-only; catches divergence but not functional bugs |
| 11 | coverage | cargo llvm-cov nextest + wasm coverage (15+ minutes!) | **Slow (900s+)** | 2 | **Y** | Major time sink due to wasm -Cinstrument-coverage recompiles; skip locally |
| 12 | snapshot-count | Python lint: .snap file count policy enforcement | Fast (<3s) | 2 | Y | Lint-only; catches review fatigue but not test failures |
| 13 | spec-compile-snippets | syn parse every fenced Rust block in spec/ | Fast (5-15s) | 4 | N | Catches spec documentation Rust syntax errors; quick & valuable |
| 14 | error-variant-coverage | Lint: all ComponentError variants appear in tests | Fast (<5s) | 4 | N | Catches untested error variants; fast & reliable |
| 15 | feature-matrix-core | ars-core: 15 feature combos (check + nextest) | Slow (60-90s) | 1 | **Y** | Feature matrix testing; rare locally, mostly CI concern |
| 16 | feature-matrix-i18n | ars-i18n: 6 combos + wasm32 check (check + nextest) | Slow (60-90s) | 1 | **Y** | Feature matrix testing; rare locally, mostly CI concern |
| 17 | feature-matrix-subsystems | 5 subsystem crates: 14 combos + wasm32 (check + nextest) | Slow (80-120s) | 1 | **Y** | Feature matrix testing; rare locally, mostly CI concern |
| 18 | feature-matrix-leptos | ars-leptos: 3 combos (check + nextest) | Medium (30-45s) | 1 | **Y** | Feature matrix testing; rarely needed locally |
| 19 | feature-matrix-dioxus | ars-dioxus: 4 combos + wasm32 (check + nextest) | Medium (40-60s) | 1 | **Y** | Feature matrix testing; rarely needed locally |

## Existing Extension Points

**Step Enum**: Declared at lines 30–98 in `/Users/ericson/.t3/worktrees/ars-ui/t3code-e16f9343/xtask/src/ci/mod.rs` as the `Step` enum with all 20 variants. The `PIPELINE_ORDER` constant at lines 104–125 defines the canonical order.

**CLI**: `/Users/ericson/.t3/worktrees/ars-ui/t3code-e16f9343/xtask/src/main.rs` (lines 18–30) has a `Ci` subcommand that accepts `steps: Vec<ci::Step>` with no `--profile` or `--fast` flag currently. The CLI already supports `cargo xci check clippy` (selective steps) via clap's `value_enum`.

**To add a fast variant**, you can either:
1. **Add a `--fast` flag** to the `Ci` subcommand in `main.rs` and resolve it in `ci::run()` to a curated subset (e.g., skip coverage + feature-matrix).
2. **Create a separate `XciFast` command** in `main.rs` that calls `ci::run()` with a fixed fast-path step list.
3. **Add a `--exclude` flag** to skip named steps dynamically.

**No git hooks exist** — no `.pre-push`, `.pre-commit`, or hook infrastructure in the repo.

## Recommended Fast Path

For a pre-push checker, run: **fmt, check, clippy, unit, integration, adapter, spec-compile-snippets, error-variant-coverage, mutual-exclusion** (9 steps, ~3–5 minutes). Skip coverage, all feature-matrix groups, browser tests, and release unless developer opts in.
```

1	# Plan: Implement task #205 — Group agnostic core
2	
3	## Context
4	
5	GitHub issue #205 (`task: Implement Group agnostic core`) is a **1-point** task in the
6	`ars-ui implementation roadmap` project. It delivers the framework-agnostic core of the
7	`Group` utility component: a stateless attribute mapper that renders a semantic grouping
8	container and exposes a `GroupContext` struct that adapters can publish to descendants for
9	`disabled`/`invalid`/`read_only` propagation.
10	
11	Authoritative spec: `spec/components/utility/group.md` (250 lines, fully specified —
12	`tier: stateless`, `foundation_deps: [architecture, accessibility]`, no shared deps).
13	
14	Status: `Todo` on the project board, no dependencies, no blockers.
15	
16	## Spec vs. issue discrepancy (RESOLVED — spec wins per CLAUDE.md "spec is the contract")
17	
18	The issue's "Tests to add first" lists two tests that contradict the spec:
19	
20	- ❌ `` `label` prop produces `aria-label` ``
21	- ❌ `` `label_id` prop produces `aria-labelledby` ``
22	
23	The spec's `Props` struct (§1.1) has exactly six fields — `id`, `disabled`, `invalid`,
24	`read_only`, `role`, `dir` — and **no** `label`/`label_id`. Spec §3.1 explicitly marks
25	`aria-label` and `aria-labelledby` as "Consumer-provided", meaning adapter users supply them
26	via prop spreading, not via Group props. Inventing `label`/`label_id` props would silently
27	diverge from the React Aria parity table (§5.1) which already shows full parity without them.
28	
29	**Decision:** Implement per spec, drop the two contradicting test bullets, replace with
30	spec-aligned tests below.
```

4	//! crate is the single source of truth for CI commands. Run the full pipeline

6

29	/// semantics.

77

165	impl Display for Error {
166	    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
167	        match self {
168	            Self::StepFailed {
169	                step,
170	                command,
171	                code,
172	            } => {
173	                write!(f, "{} failed", step_name(*step))?;
174

177	                }

179	                write!(f, ": {command}")
180	            }
181	
182	            Self::MissingTool { tool, install_hint } => {
183	                write!(
184	                    f,
185	                    "missing required tool: {tool}\n  install: {install_hint}"
186	                )
187	            }
188	
189	            Self::Io(e) => write!(f, "IO error: {e}"),
190	
191	            Self::Coverage(e) => write!(f, "{e}"),
192	
193	            Self::Lint(e) => write!(f, "{e}"),
194

196	        }

202	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
203	// Public entry point
204	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
205	
206	/// Run the specified CI steps, or the full pipeline if none are given.
207	///
208	/// Execution is sequential and fail-fast: the first step that fails stops the
209	/// pipeline and returns its error.
210	///
211	/// # Errors
212	///
213	/// Returns [`CiError`] on the first step that fails — either a subprocess
214	/// non-zero exit, a missing tool, or a coverage threshold violation.
215	pub fn run(steps: Vec<Step>, message_format: Option<&str>) -> Result<(), Error> {
216	    let steps = resolve_steps(steps);
217	
218	    for (i, step) in steps.iter().enumerate() {
219	        print_header(*step, i + 1, steps.len());
220	        run_step(*step, message_format)?;
221	        print_pass(*step);
222	    }
223	
224	    print_summary(&steps);
225	
226	    Ok(())
227	}
228	
229	/// Format the workspace in place.
230	///
231	/// Runs `leptosfmt` over Leptos adapter/example code first so `view!`
232	/// macro bodies and Tailwind class strings are normalized, runs `dx fmt`
233	/// over Dioxus adapter/example code so `rsx!` macro bodies are normalized,
234	/// then runs `cargo +nightly fmt --all` for the Rust workspace.
235	///
236	/// # Errors
237	///
238	/// Returns [`Error`] when a required formatter is missing or a formatter
239	/// command exits non-zero.
240	pub fn format_workspace() -> Result<(), Error> {
241	    preflight_nightly()?;
242	    preflight_leptosfmt()?;
243	    preflight_dioxus_cli()?;
244	
245	    leptosfmt_format()?;
246	    dioxusfmt(false)?;
247	
248	    cargo(Step::Fmt, &["+nightly", "fmt", "--all"])
249	}
250	
251	/// Format a single Rust source buffer from stdin to stdout.
252	///
253	/// This is the rustfmt-compatible entrypoint used by editor integrations.
254	/// It detects Leptos `view!` and Dioxus `rsx!` buffers and routes them

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/.claude/plans/implement-ta[REDACTED_SK].md",
  "old_string": "| Coverage thresholds | `xtask/src/coverage.rs` (ars-components: 90/90) |",
  "new_string": "| Coverage thresholds | `xtask/src/coverage.rs` (ars-components: 90/90) |\n\n---\n\n# Plan: Lighter local `cargo xci` (`--profile fast`)\n\n## Context\n\n`cargo xci` is the local mirror of `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s 20 gates. We just measured it\ntaking ~25 minutes locally — the bulk of that is the `coverage` step (~15 minutes; wasm32\n`-Cinstrument-coverage` recompile of every adapter crate via nightly + LLVM 22) and the\nfive `feature-matrix-*` steps (~5 minutes combined). After a normal implementation flow the\nrelevant unit / integration / adapter test suites have already run during TDD iteration and\nthe `post-implementation-audit` skill; re-running them under coverage measurement before push\nis pure redundancy. Five different feature-matrix combos checking subsystem crates the change\ndidn't touch is also overkill for a local gate whose only job is \"catch what focused tests\nmiss before this push lands on origin\".\n\nWe need an opt-in **fast profile** that covers cross-crate / workspace-level regressions\nwithout paying the wasm-coverage and feature-matrix taxes. Target run time: ~5 minutes on a\nwarm cache.\n\n## Step selection\n\nPer the Phase-1 audit of `xtask/src/ci/mod.rs` lines 30–125, the 20 steps split cleanly:\n\n**Fast profile — 10 steps, ~5 min:**\n\n| Step | Why it stays |\n|---|---|\n| `Fmt` | Catches formatter drift across the workspace; ~15 s. |\n| `Check` | Type-error gate; fast and foundational. |\n| `Clippy` | `-D warnings` gate; required for merge. |\n| `Unit` | Workspace-wide nextest run; catches inter-crate regressions focused tests miss. |\n| `Integration` | Black-box suite; separate from unit, fast enough, catches real bugs. |\n| `Adapter` | Leptos + Dioxus harness; touches both adapters in one pass. |\n| `SpecCompileSnippets` | Catches spec drift Codex can't see; fast. |\n| `ErrorVariantCoverage` | Catches untested `ComponentError` variants; fast lint. |\n| `MutualExclusion` | The `icu4x` + `web-intl` compile-guard; ~3 s and load-bearing. |\n| `SnapshotCount` | Per-component snapshot policy lint; ~3 s. |\n\n**Skipped from fast — 10 steps, ~20 min saved:**\n\n| Step | Why it's skipped from fast (still runs in `cargo xci` full) |\n|---|---|\n| `I18nBrowser`, `DomBrowser` | wasm-pack browser runs, only relevant when ars-i18n / ars-dom code changes; skip-by-default and re-add positionally when you touched those crates. |\n| `Release` | Release-profile compile/test; release-only failures are rare. |\n| `AdapterParity` | Per-component test-count diff lint; useful but not pre-push critical. |\n| `Coverage` | The big one — wasm `-Cinstrument-coverage` recompile + nightly + LLVM 22. The same tests already ran in `Unit`/`Integration`/`Adapter`; redundant pre-push. CI still enforces thresholds. |\n| `FeatureMatrixCore`, `FeatureMatrixI18n`, `FeatureMatrixSubsystems`, `FeatureMatrixLeptos`, `FeatureMatrixDioxus` | Feature-combination CI concern; rarely catches anything a workspace-wide `--all-features` `check`/`clippy`/`unit` pass didn't already. |\n\nPower users who touched wasm/feature code can opt the skipped steps back in positionally:\n`cargo xci --profile fast i18n-browser dom-browser`. Positional steps override the profile\n(preserves the current `cargo xci fmt check` selection semantics).\n\n## Files\n\n### Modify\n\n- **`xtask/src/ci/mod.rs`**:\n  - Add `pub enum Profile { Full, Fast }` with `Default = Full` derive next to the `Step` enum\n    (~line 30).\n  - Add `const FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER: &[Step]` next to `PIPELINE_ORDER` (~line 104), listing the\n    10 steps above in dependency order: `Fmt, Check, Clippy, Unit, Integration, Adapter,\n    SpecCompileSnippets, ErrorVariantCoverage, MutualExclusion, SnapshotCount`.\n  - Change `pub fn run(steps: Vec<Step>, message_format: Option<&str>) -> Result<(), Error>`\n    to `pub fn run(steps: Vec<Step>, profile: Profile, message_format: Option<&str>) ->\n    Result<(), Error>`.\n  - Change `resolve_steps(vec) -> Vec<Step>` to `resolve_steps(vec, profile) -> Vec<Step>`:\n    - If positional `vec` is non-empty, return as-is (existing behavior).\n    - Else return `FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER.to_vec()` when `profile == Profile::Fast`, else\n      `PIPELINE_ORDER.to_vec()`.\n\n- **`xtask/src/main.rs`** lines 18–30:\n  - Add `#[arg(long, value_enum, default_value_t = ci::Profile::Full)] profile: ci::Profile`\n    to the `Command::Ci` variant.\n  - Update the call site to pass `profile` through to `ci::run`.\n  - Update the doc comment on `Ci` to mention `--profile fast` and the ~5-minute target.\n\n- **`.cargo/config.toml`** — add one alias after `xci`:\n  ```toml\n  xci-fast = \"xtask ci --profile fast\"\n  ```\n  So both `cargo xci --profile fast` and `cargo xci-fast` work.\n\n- **`AGENTS.md` (the real file behind the `CLAUDE.md` symlink), step 9**:\n  - Reword to: \"After user approval, run `cargo xci --profile fast` locally (~5 min) before\n    every push. Reserve full `cargo xci` (~25 min) for the final pre-PR run or after\n    substantial refactors that may have shifted feature-flag interactions. For small\n    follow-up changes, run the focused tests and checks that cover the edited code instead.\"\n\n### Add tests\n\nAdd a `mod tests` block at the bottom of `xtask/src/ci/mod.rs`:\n\n- `profile_default_is_full` — `Profile::default() == Profile::Full`.\n- `resolve_steps_fast_returns_fast_pipeline` — `resolve_steps(vec![], Profile::Fast)`\n  equals `FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER`.\n- `resolve_steps_full_returns_full_pipeline` — `resolve_steps(vec![], Profile::Full)`\n  equals `PIPELINE_ORDER`.\n- `resolve_steps_positional_overrides_profile` — `resolve_steps(vec![Step::Fmt],\n  Profile::Fast)` equals `vec![Step::Fmt]`.\n- `fast_pipeline_is_subset_of_full` — every `Step` in `FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER` also appears in\n  `PIPELINE_ORDER` (defensive: prevents the fast list from referencing a step that's been\n  removed from the full pipeline).\n- `fast_pipeline_excludes_coverage_and_feature_matrix` — guards the intent: the fast list\n  must not contain `Coverage` or any `FeatureMatrix*` variant. Catches a regression where\n  someone reflexively adds them back without reading the rationale.\n\n## Verification\n\n1. `cargo build -p xtask` — clean.\n2. `cargo test -p xtask` — new unit tests pass.\n3. `cargo xci --profile fast` (warm cache) — runs the 10 steps, exits green, wall-clock ≤\n   6 minutes.\n4. `cargo xci --help` — surfaces the new `--profile` flag and its values.\n5. `cargo xci` (no args, no flag) — still runs all 20 steps (backward compat).\n6. `cargo xci --profile fast fmt check` — runs only `fmt` + `check` (positional override\n   still wins).\n7. `cargo xci-fast` — alias dispatches correctly.\n8. Spot-check on `main` after introducing a known break (e.g., temporarily mismatch\n   `aria-disabled` value in a stateless component) and confirm the fast profile catches\n   it via `Unit` failure within ~3 minutes.\n\n## Out of scope\n\n- A `--exclude <step>` flag for ad-hoc subtraction from a profile. Useful but deferrable; add\n  as a follow-up if the fast profile turns out to need finer-grained subtraction.\n- A pre-push git hook that auto-runs `cargo xci-fast`. Possible follow-up, but enforcement\n  here belongs in CLAUDE.md / habit, not a hook that surprises devs.\n- Promoting `--profile fast` to the new `cargo xci` no-arg default. Backward compat with\n  any external scripts that call `cargo xci` argues for keeping `full` as the no-arg\n  default. # Plan: Implement task #205 — Group agnostic core

## Context

GitHub issue #205 (`task: Implement Group agnostic core`) is a **1-point** task in the
`ars-ui implementation roadmap` project. It delivers the framework-agnostic core of the
`Group` utility component: a stateless attribute mapper that renders a semantic grouping
container and exposes a `GroupContext` struct that adapters can publish to descendants for
`disabled`/`invalid`/`read_only` propagation.

Authoritative spec: `spec/components/utility/group.md` (250 lines, fully specified —
`tier: stateless`, `foundation_deps: [architecture, accessibility]`, no shared deps).

Status: `Todo` on the project board, no dependencies, no blockers.

## Spec vs. issue discrepancy (RESOLVED — spec wins per CLAUDE.md "spec is the contract")

The issue's "Tests to add first" lists two tests that contradict the spec:

- ❌ `` `label` prop produces `aria-label` ``
- ❌ `` `label_id` prop produces `aria-labelledby` ``

The spec's `Props` struct (§1.1) has exactly six fields — `id`, `disabled`, `invalid`,
`read_only`, `role`, `dir` — and **no** `label`/`label_id`. Spec §3.1 explicitly marks
`aria-label` and `aria-labelledby` as "Consumer-provided", meaning adapter users supply them
via prop spreading, not via Group props. Inventing `label`/`label_id` props would silently
diverge from the React Aria parity table (§5.1) which already shows full parity without them.

**Decision:** Implement per spec, drop the two contradicting test bullets, replace with
spec-aligned tests below.

## Internal spec inconsistency — resolved on accessibility merits

Spec §3.1 prose said: "When `role` is `Presentation`, state attributes (`aria-disabled`,
`aria-invalid`) are omitted since the grouping has no semantic meaning."

Spec §1.2 code example: unconditionally emits state attributes regardless of `role`.

**Best-outcome decision: always emit state attributes when the prop is true, regardless of
role.** Reasoning:

1. **WAI-ARIA 1.2 §5.4** explicitly states `aria-*` global states and properties ARE still
   exposed on elements with `role="presentation"` — the role only suppresses the *implicit*
   role mapping, not state attributes. The original §3.1 prose was based on a misreading.
2. **React Aria parity** (§5.1 documents full parity): React Aria emits state attributes
   unconditionally on its `Group`. Diverging here would silently break parity that the spec
   commits to.
3. **`GroupContext` is the load-bearing propagation mechanism**, not root ARIA. The root
   `aria-disabled`/`aria-invalid`/`aria-readonly` are decorative complements that help AT
   when the disabled state visually applies to the whole group; gating them on `role`
   creates asymmetric behavior between the propagation path (always works) and the AT
   announcement (sometimes works).
4. **`data-ars-*` styling hooks must always emit** regardless of role so CSS targeting stays
   consistent. Splitting aria-* vs data-* on role-gated logic introduces a footgun.
5. **Simpler mental model**: `disabled=true ⇒ aria-disabled on root`, end of story. Fewer
   branches, fewer edge cases, lower test surface.

**Implementation consequence:** Match the §1.2 code example. **Spec consequence:** Replace
the §3.1 line "When `role` is `Presentation`, state attributes (`aria-disabled`,
`aria-invalid`) are omitted..." with a brief note clarifying that state attributes are
always emitted (and citing the React Aria parity / WAI-ARIA §5.4 justification). Lands in
the same PR per CLAUDE.md "no spec drift" rule.

## Files

### Create

- **`crates/ars-components/src/utility/group.rs`** — entire component module (Props, GroupRole,
  GroupContext, Part, Api, ConnectApi impl, and `#[cfg(test)] mod tests`). Template:
  `crates/ars-components/src/utility/separator.rs` (446 lines, identical structure minus the
  Context struct). No state machine, no Service, no Messages.

- **`spec/components/utility/group.md` §3.1** — replace the "When `role` is `Presentation`,
  state attributes are omitted..." line with a one-line note that state attributes are always
  emitted, citing WAI-ARIA 1.2 §5.4 and the React Aria parity commitment in §5.1. Keeps the

### Snapshots (created by `cargo insta accept` after first test run)

- `crates/ars-components/src/utility/snapshots/ars_components__utility__group__tests__*.snap`

## Implementation outline

Match `spec/components/utility/group.md` §1.1 and §1.2 line-for-line. Specifically:

1. **`GroupRole`** — `#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Default)]` enum with three
   variants (`Group` default, `Region`, `Presentation`). Use `#[default]` on `Group`.

2. **`Props`** — `#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, PartialEq, HasId)]`. The spec writes a manual
   `impl Default` that produces identical output to derive; use derive for DRY (matches
   `Separator`'s convention). Builder chain matching separator's pattern: `new()`, `id()`,
   `disabled()`, `invalid()`, `read_only()`, `role()`, `dir()`. Setters that don't take
   `String` are `const fn`.

3. **`GroupContext`** — `#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]` plain struct. Three `pub bool`
   fields exactly as spec §1.2. Named `GroupContext` (not `Context`) per foundation 08 §8.4
   ("Secondary contexts or helpers use descriptive non-prefixed names — `GroupContext`,
   `QueueContext`, ..."), so consumers write `group::GroupContext`.

4. **`Part`** — `#[derive(ComponentPart)] #[scope = "group"] pub enum Part { Root }`.

5. **`Api`** — `#[derive(Clone, Debug)] pub struct Api { props: Props }`. Methods:
   `new(Props) -> Self` (`const`), `props() -> &Props`, `id() -> &str`,
   `group_context() -> GroupContext`, `root_attrs() -> AttrMap`. `root_attrs` follows the
   spec code: set Id, scope/part data attrs, then a `match` on role emitting
   `role="group"|"region"|"presentation"`, then three independent `if` blocks emitting
   `aria-disabled` + `data-ars-disabled`, `aria-invalid` + `data-ars-invalid`,
   `aria-readonly` + `data-ars-readonly`, then optional `dir`.

6. **`ConnectApi for Api`** — single-arm `match part { Part::Root => self.root_attrs() }`.

## Tests (TDD — write before implementation per workspace convention)

Drop the issue's two label-related test bullets. The replacement coverage that fully
exercises the spec contract:

**Props / Api / Debug smoke**

- `props_default_is_zero_state` — id="", disabled/invalid/read_only=false, role=Group, dir=None.
- `props_builder_round_trips` — chained setters set exactly their own fields; `Props::new()
   == Props::default()`.
- `props_has_id_derive_round_trips` — `with_id` / `set_id` / `HasId::id` round-trip.
- `props_clone_and_partial_eq_round_trip`.
- `props_and_api_debug_impl_is_non_empty`.
- `api_id_returns_empty_str_for_default_props`.
- `api_exposes_props_fields`.

**Role mapping** (covers issue bullet 1 & 2)

- `root_default_role_is_group` — `Api::new(Props::default()).root_attrs()` →
  `role="group"`.
- `root_role_region_overrides` — `role(GroupRole::Region)` → `role="region"`.
- `root_role_presentation_overrides` — `role(GroupRole::Presentation)` → `role="presentation"`.
- `role_branches_produce_different_role_attrs` — cross-branch inequality
  (defensive regression).

**State propagation via `group_context()`** (covers issue bullet 3)

- `group_context_propagates_disabled` — `Props { disabled: true, ..default }` →
  `Api::group_context() == GroupContext { disabled: true, invalid: false, read_only: false }`.
- `group_context_propagates_invalid` — analogous.
- `group_context_propagates_read_only` — analogous.
- `group_context_default_is_all_false`.

**Root state attributes**

- `root_disabled_emits_aria_and_data_attrs` — `aria-disabled="true"` + `data-ars-disabled` set.
- `root_invalid_emits_aria_and_data_attrs` — `aria-invalid="true"` + `data-ars-invalid` set.
- `root_read_only_emits_aria_and_data_attrs` — `aria-readonly="true"` + `data-ars-readonly` set.
- `root_state_attrs_omitted_when_false` — none of the three are present in the default-props
  AttrMap.
- `root_dir_emits_dir_attr` — `dir(Some(Direction::Rtl))` → `dir="rtl"`; default → no `dir`.
- `root_id_is_set_to_props_id` — even when empty, follows spec §1.2 code which sets
  unconditionally.

**ConnectApi dispatch**

- `part_attrs_dispatches_root` — sanity check (scope/part data attrs match).
- `part_attrs_root_equals_root_attrs` — loop across role × disabled × invalid × read_only ×
  dir combinations and assert `api.part_attrs(Part::Root) == api.root_attrs()`.

**Snapshots** (one per output-affecting branch group, named per the
`snapshot_attrs(&attr_map)` convention from separator.rs)

- `group_root_default` (role=Group, no state)
- `group_root_region` (role=Region, no state)
- `group_root_presentation` (role=Presentation, no state)
- `group_root_all_states` (role=Group, disabled+invalid+read_only=true)
- `group_root_dir_rtl` (role=Group, dir=Rtl)

6. **Run `post-implementation-audit` skill** (mandatory per CLAUDE.md §7) — three sequential
   audits: (a) spec/impl drift incl. the §3.1 prose fix above, (b) iterative "anything else
   missing?" rounds (≥2), (c) test-coverage audit across unit + snapshot + doctest dimensions.
   Every finding lands in this PR.
7. `cargo xci` — full pre-PR gate; must pass before opening the PR.
8. Present diff to user → after approval, commit, push, open PR with `Closes #205` in the
   body, attach `snapshot-reviewed` label.

# Plan: Lighter local `cargo xci` (`--profile fast`)

## Context

`cargo xci` is the local mirror of `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s 20 gates. We just measured it
taking ~25 minutes locally — the bulk of that is the `coverage` step (~15 minutes; wasm32
`-Cinstrument-coverage` recompile of every adapter crate via nightly + LLVM 22) and the
five `feature-matrix-*` steps (~5 minutes combined). After a normal implementation flow the
relevant unit / integration / adapter test suites have already run during TDD iteration and
the `post-implementation-audit` skill; re-running them under coverage measurement before push
is pure redundancy. Five different feature-matrix combos checking subsystem crates the change
didn't touch is also overkill for a local gate whose only job is "catch what focused tests
miss before this push lands on origin".

We need an opt-in **fast profile** that covers cross-crate / workspace-level regressions
without paying the wasm-coverage and feature-matrix taxes. Target run time: ~5 minutes on a
warm cache.

## Step selection

Per the Phase-1 audit of `xtask/src/ci/mod.rs` lines 30–125, the 20 steps split cleanly:

**Fast profile — 10 steps, ~5 min:**

| Step | Why it stays |
|---|---|
| `Fmt` | Catches formatter drift across the workspace; ~15 s. |
| `Check` | Type-error gate; fast and foundational. |
| `Clippy` | `-D warnings` gate; required for merge. |
| `Unit` | Workspace-wide nextest run; catches inter-crate regressions focused tests miss. |
| `Integration` | Black-box suite; separate from unit, fast enough, catches real bugs. |
| `Adapter` | Leptos + Dioxus harness; touches both adapters in one pass. |
| `SpecCompileSnippets` | Catches spec drift Codex can't see; fast. |
| `ErrorVariantCoverage` | Catches untested `ComponentError` variants; fast lint. |
| `MutualExclusion` | The `icu4x` + `web-intl` compile-guard; ~3 s and load-bearing. |
| `SnapshotCount` | Per-component snapshot policy lint; ~3 s. |

**Skipped from fast — 10 steps, ~20 min saved:**

| Step | Why it's skipped from fast (still runs in `cargo xci` full) |
|---|---|
| `I18nBrowser`, `DomBrowser` | wasm-pack browser runs, only relevant when ars-i18n / ars-dom code changes; skip-by-default and re-add positionally when you touched those crates. |
| `Release` | Release-profile compile/test; release-only failures are rare. |
| `AdapterParity` | Per-component test-count diff lint; useful but not pre-push critical. |
| `Coverage` | The big one — wasm `-Cinstrument-coverage` recompile + nightly + LLVM 22. The same tests already ran in `Unit`/`Integration`/`Adapter`; redundant pre-push. CI still enforces thresholds. |
| `FeatureMatrixCore`, `FeatureMatrixI18n`, `FeatureMatrixSubsystems`, `FeatureMatrixLeptos`, `FeatureMatrixDioxus` | Feature-combination CI concern; rarely catches anything a workspace-wide `--all-features` `check`/`clippy`/`unit` pass didn't already. |

Power users who touched wasm/feature code can opt the skipped steps back in positionally:
`cargo xci --profile fast i18n-browser dom-browser`. Positional steps override the profile
(preserves the current `cargo xci fmt check` selection semantics).

## Files

### Modify

- **`xtask/src/ci/mod.rs`**:
  - Add `pub enum Profile { Full, Fast }` with `Default = Full` derive next to the `Step` enum
    (~line 30).
  - Add `const FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER: &[Step]` next to `PIPELINE_ORDER` (~line 104), listing the
    10 steps above in dependency order: `Fmt, Check, Clippy, Unit, Integration, Adapter,
    SpecCompileSnippets, ErrorVariantCoverage, MutualExclusion, SnapshotCount`.
  - Change `pub fn run(steps: Vec<Step>, message_format: Option<&str>) -> Result<(), Error>`
    to `pub fn run(steps: Vec<Step>, profile: Profile, message_format: Option<&str>) ->
    Result<(), Error>`.
  - Change `resolve_steps(vec) -> Vec<Step>` to `resolve_steps(vec, profile) -> Vec<Step>`:
    - If positional `vec` is non-empty, return as-is (existing behavior).
    - Else return `FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER.to_vec()` when `profile == Profile::Fast`, else
      `PIPELINE_ORDER.to_vec()`.

- **`xtask/src/main.rs`** lines 18–30:
  - Add `#[arg(long, value_enum, default_value_t = ci::Profile::Full)] profile: ci::Profile`
    to the `Command::Ci` variant.
  - Update the call site to pass `profile` through to `ci::run`.
  - Update the doc comment on `Ci` to mention `--profile fast` and the ~5-minute target.

- **`.cargo/config.toml`** — add one alias after `xci`:
  ```toml
  xci-fast = "xtask ci --profile fast"
  ```
  So both `cargo xci --profile fast` and `cargo xci-fast` work.

- **`AGENTS.md` (the real file behind the `CLAUDE.md` symlink), step 9**:
  - Reword to: "After user approval, run `cargo xci --profile fast` locally (~5 min) before
    every push. Reserve full `cargo xci` (~25 min) for the final pre-PR run or after
    substantial refactors that may have shifted feature-flag interactions. For small
    follow-up changes, run the focused tests and checks that cover the edited code instead."

### Add tests

Add a `mod tests` block at the bottom of `xtask/src/ci/mod.rs`:

- `profile_default_is_full` — `Profile::default() == Profile::Full`.
- `resolve_steps_fast_returns_fast_pipeline` — `resolve_steps(vec![], Profile::Fast)`
  equals `FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER`.
- `resolve_steps_full_returns_full_pipeline` — `resolve_steps(vec![], Profile::Full)`
  equals `PIPELINE_ORDER`.
- `resolve_steps_positional_overrides_profile` — `resolve_steps(vec![Step::Fmt],
  Profile::Fast)` equals `vec![Step::Fmt]`.
- `fast_pipeline_is_subset_of_full` — every `Step` in `FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER` also appears in
  `PIPELINE_ORDER` (defensive: prevents the fast list from referencing a step that's been
  removed from the full pipeline).
- `fast_pipeline_excludes_coverage_and_feature_matrix` — guards the intent: the fast list
  must not contain `Coverage` or any `FeatureMatrix*` variant. Catches a regression where
  someone reflexively adds them back without reading the rationale.

## Verification

1. `cargo build -p xtask` — clean.
2. `cargo test -p xtask` — new unit tests pass.
3. `cargo xci --profile fast` (warm cache) — runs the 10 steps, exits green, wall-clock ≤
   6 minutes.
4. `cargo xci --help` — surfaces the new `--profile` flag and its values.
5. `cargo xci` (no args, no flag) — still runs all 20 steps (backward compat).
6. `cargo xci --profile fast fmt check` — runs only `fmt` + `check` (positional override
   still wins).
7. `cargo xci-fast` — alias dispatches correctly.
8. Spot-check on `main` after introducing a known break (e.g., temporarily mismatch
   `aria-disabled` value in a stateless component) and confirm the fast profile catches
   it via `Unit` failure within ~3 minutes.

## Out of scope

- A `--exclude <step>` flag for ad-hoc subtraction from a profile. Useful but deferrable; add
  as a follow-up if the fast profile turns out to need finer-grained subtraction.
- A pre-push git hook that auto-runs `cargo xci-fast`. Possible follow-up, but enforcement
  here belongs in CLAUDE.md / habit, not a hook that surprises devs.
- Promoting `--profile fast` to the new `cargo xci` no-arg default. Backward compat with
  any external scripts that call `cargo xci` argues for keeping `full` as the no-arg
  default. The user can flip later if telemetry shows nobody uses the full local run.

| Cargo aliases | `.cargo/config.toml` |
| Workflow doc to update | `AGENTS.md` step 9 (symlinked as `CLAUDE.md`) |

206	/// Run the specified CI steps, or the full pipeline if none are given.
207	///
208	/// Execution is sequential and fail-fast: the first step that fails stops the
209	/// pipeline and returns its error.
210	///
211	/// # Errors
212	///
213	/// Returns [`CiError`] on the first step that fails — either a subprocess
214	/// non-zero exit, a missing tool, or a coverage threshold violation.

216	    let steps = resolve_steps(steps);
217	
218	    for (i, step) in steps.iter().enumerate() {
219	        print_header(*step, i + 1, steps.len());
220	        run_step(*step, message_format)?;
221	        print_pass(*step);
222	    }
223	
224	    print_summary(&steps);
225	
226	    Ok(())
227	}

278	    } else if is_dioxus_source(&input) {

297	/// Resolve the user-provided step list into the concrete list to execute.
298	///
299	/// - Empty input → full [`PIPELINE_ORDER`].
300	/// - `FeatureMatrix` → expands to the five individual groups.
301	/// - Everything else passes through as-is.
302	fn resolve_steps(steps: Vec<Step>) -> Vec<Step> {
303	    if steps.is_empty() {
304	        return PIPELINE_ORDER.to_vec();
305	    }
306	
307	    let mut resolved = Vec::with_capacity(steps.len());
308	
309	    for step in steps {
310	        if step == Step::FeatureMatrix {
311	            resolved.extend_from_slice(&[
312	                Step::FeatureMatrixCore,
313	                Step::FeatureMatrixI18n,
314	                Step::FeatureMatrixSubsystems,
315	                Step::FeatureMatrixLeptos,
316	                Step::FeatureMatrixDioxus,
317	            ]);
318	        } else {
319	            resolved.push(step);
320	        }
321	    }
322	
323	    resolved
324	}

  "old_string": "    /// Verify the mutual-exclusion compile guard for ars-i18n backend features.\n    MutualExclusion,\n}\n\n/// Default pipeline order when no steps are specified.\n///\n/// `FeatureMatrix` is intentionally absent — the pipeline runs each sub-group\n/// individually so progress is visible per group.\nconst PIPELINE_ORDER: &[Step] = &[\n    Step::Fmt,\n    Step::Check,\n    Step::Clippy,\n    Step::Unit,\n    Step::I18nBrowser,\n    Step::DomBrowser,\n    Step::Release,\n    Step::Integration,\n    Step::Adapter,\n    Step::AdapterParity,\n    Step::Coverage,\n    Step::SnapshotCount,\n    Step::SpecCompileSnippets,\n    Step::ErrorVariantCoverage,\n    Step::FeatureMatrixCore,\n    Step::FeatureMatrixI18n,\n    Step::FeatureMatrixSubsystems,\n    Step::FeatureMatrixLeptos,\n    Step::FeatureMatrixDioxus,\n    Step::MutualExclusion,\n];",
  "new_string": "    /// Verify the mutual-exclusion compile guard for ars-i18n backend features.\n    MutualExclusion,\n}\n\n/// Pipeline profile selecting which step list to run when no positional steps\n/// are passed.\n///\n/// - [`Profile::Full`] mirrors the GitHub Actions CI job exactly (20 gates,\n///   ~25 minutes locally) and is the default for backward compatibility with\n///   scripts that call `cargo xci` directly.\n/// - [`Profile::Fast`] runs the curated [`FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER`] subset\n///   (~5 minutes) intended as the routine pre-push gate. It drops the coverage\n///   recompile, the wasm browser tests, the release-profile checks, and the\n///   five `feature-matrix-*` groups — all of which are CI-only concerns that\n///   re-test what `Unit` / `Integration` / `Adapter` already covered.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Default, clap::ValueEnum)]\n#[value(rename_all = \"kebab-case\")]\npub enum Profile {\n    /// Full CI parity (default). Runs every step in [`PIPELINE_ORDER`].\n    #[default]\n    Full,\n    /// Fast pre-push gate. Runs every step in [`FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER`].\n    Fast,\n}\n\n/// Default pipeline order when no steps are specified and [`Profile::Full`]\n/// is selected.\n///\n/// `FeatureMatrix` is intentionally absent — the pipeline runs each sub-group\n/// individually so progress is visible per group.\nconst PIPELINE_ORDER: &[Step] = &[\n    Step::Fmt,\n    Step::Check,\n    Step::Clippy,\n    Step::Unit,\n    Step::I18nBrowser,\n    Step::DomBrowser,\n    Step::Release,\n    Step::Integration,\n    Step::Adapter,\n    Step::AdapterParity,\n    Step::Coverage,\n    Step::SnapshotCount,\n    Step::SpecCompileSnippets,\n    Step::ErrorVariantCoverage,\n    Step::FeatureMatrixCore,\n    Step::FeatureMatrixI18n,\n    Step::FeatureMatrixSubsystems,\n    Step::FeatureMatrixLeptos,\n    Step::FeatureMatrixDioxus,\n    Step::MutualExclusion,\n];\n\n/// Curated pipeline order when [`Profile::Fast`] is selected and no positional\n/// steps are passed.\n///\n/// Covers cross-crate / workspace-level regressions and the structural lints\n/// that gate merge, while skipping the wasm-coverage recompile, the wasm\n/// browser tests, the release-profile checks, and the five `feature-matrix-*`\n/// groups — those run in CI and rarely change the outcome of a local pre-push\n/// gate when `Unit`, `Integration`, `Adapter`, and `--all-features check`\n/// have already passed.\n///\n/// Power users who touched wasm or feature-gated code can opt the skipped\n/// steps back in positionally, e.g. `cargo xci --profile fast i18n-browser`;\n/// positional steps still override the profile.\nconst FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER: &[Step] = &[\n    Step::Fmt,\n    Step::Check,\n    Step::Clippy,\n    Step::Unit,\n    Step::Integration,\n    Step::Adapter,\n    Step::SpecCompileSnippets,\n    Step::ErrorVariantCoverage,\n    Step::MutualExclusion,\n    Step::SnapshotCount,\n];"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/.t3/worktrees/ars-ui/t3code-e16f9343/xtask/src/ci/mod.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Run the specified CI steps, or the full pipeline if none are given.\n///\n/// Execution is sequential and fail-fast: the first step that fails stops the\n/// pipeline and returns its error.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// Returns [`CiError`] on the first step that fails — either a subprocess\n/// non-zero exit, a missing tool, or a coverage threshold violation.\npub fn run(steps: Vec<Step>, message_format: Option<&str>) -> Result<(), Error> {\n    let steps = resolve_steps(steps);\n\n    for (i, step) in steps.iter().enumerate() {\n        print_header(*step, i + 1, steps.len());\n        run_step(*step, message_format)?;\n        print_pass(*step);\n    }\n\n    print_summary(&steps);\n\n    Ok(())\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Run the specified CI steps.\n///\n/// When `steps` is empty, the step list is taken from the `profile`'s default\n/// pipeline ([`PIPELINE_ORDER`] for [`Profile::Full`], [`FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER`]\n/// for [`Profile::Fast`]). When `steps` is non-empty, the positional list\n/// runs as-is and `profile` is ignored — this preserves the existing\n/// `cargo xci fmt check` ad-hoc selection semantics.\n///\n/// Execution is sequential and fail-fast: the first step that fails stops the\n/// pipeline and returns its error.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// Returns [`Error`] on the first step that fails — either a subprocess\n/// non-zero exit, a missing tool, or a coverage threshold violation.\npub fn run(\n    steps: Vec<Step>,\n    profile: Profile,\n    message_format: Option<&str>,\n) -> Result<(), Error> {\n    let steps = resolve_steps(steps, profile);\n\n    for (i, step) in steps.iter().enumerate() {\n        print_header(*step, i + 1, steps.len());\n        run_step(*step, message_format)?;\n        print_pass(*step);\n    }\n\n    print_summary(&steps);\n\n    Ok(())\n}"
}

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/.t3/worktrees/ars-ui/t3code-e16f9343/xtask/src/ci/mod.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Resolve the user-provided step list into the concrete list to execute.\n///\n/// - Empty input → full [`PIPELINE_ORDER`].\n/// - `FeatureMatrix` → expands to the five individual groups.\n/// - Everything else passes through as-is.\nfn resolve_steps(steps: Vec<Step>) -> Vec<Step> {\n    if steps.is_empty() {\n        return PIPELINE_ORDER.to_vec();\n    }\n\n    let mut resolved = Vec::with_capacity(steps.len());\n\n    for step in steps {\n        if step == Step::FeatureMatrix {\n            resolved.extend_from_slice(&[\n                Step::FeatureMatrixCore,\n                Step::FeatureMatrixI18n,\n                Step::FeatureMatrixSubsystems,\n                Step::FeatureMatrixLeptos,\n                Step::FeatureMatrixDioxus,\n            ]);\n        } else {\n            resolved.push(step);\n        }\n    }\n\n    resolved\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Resolve the user-provided step list into the concrete list to execute.\n///\n/// - Empty input → profile's default pipeline ([`PIPELINE_ORDER`] for\n///   [`Profile::Full`], [`FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER`] for [`Profile::Fast`]).\n/// - `FeatureMatrix` → expands to the five individual groups.\n/// - Everything else passes through as-is.\n///\n/// Positional steps always override the profile: passing `vec![Step::Fmt]`\n/// with `Profile::Fast` yields `vec![Step::Fmt]`, not the fast subset.\nfn resolve_steps(steps: Vec<Step>, profile: Profile) -> Vec<Step> {\n    if steps.is_empty() {\n        return match profile {\n            Profile::Full => PIPELINE_ORDER.to_vec(),\n            Profile::Fast => FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER.to_vec(),\n        };\n    }\n\n    let mut resolved = Vec::with_capacity(steps.len());\n\n    for step in steps {\n        if step == Step::FeatureMatrix {\n            resolved.extend_from_slice(&[\n                Step::FeatureMatrixCore,\n                Step::FeatureMatrixI18n,\n                Step::FeatureMatrixSubsystems,\n                Step::FeatureMatrixLeptos,\n                Step::FeatureMatrixDioxus,\n            ]);\n        } else {\n            resolved.push(step);\n        }\n    }\n\n    resolved\n}"
}

  "new_string": "    /// Run CI pipeline steps locally (alias: `cargo xci`).\n    ///\n    /// `--profile full` (default) mirrors GitHub Actions exactly — all 20 gates,\n    /// ~25 min locally. `--profile fast` runs the curated pre-push subset\n    /// (~5 min): fmt, check, clippy, unit, integration, adapter,\n    /// spec-compile-snippets, error-variant-coverage, mutual-exclusion,\n    /// snapshot-count. Positional `steps` override the profile and run as-is,\n    /// so `cargo xci fmt check` still works.\n    Ci {\n        /// Steps to run (default: every step in the selected profile).\n        ///\n        /// Use `feature-matrix` to run all five feature-flag groups at once.\n        /// When supplied, this list overrides `--profile`.\n        #[arg(value_enum)]\n        steps: Vec<ci::Step>,\n\n        /// Pipeline profile when no positional steps are given. Defaults to\n        /// `full` for backward compatibility; use `fast` (alias:\n        /// `cargo xci-fast`) for the routine pre-push gate.\n        #[arg(long, value_enum, default_value_t = ci::Profile::Full)]\n        profile: ci::Profile,\n\n        /// Forward `--message-format` to cargo check/clippy (e.g. `json` for\n        /// rust-analyzer's `overrideCommand`).\n        #[arg(long)]\n        message_format: Option<String>,\n    },"
}

  "old_string": "    #[test]\n    fn empty_steps_resolve_to_pipeline_order() {\n        let resolved = resolve_steps(vec![]);\n\n        assert_eq!(resolved, PIPELINE_ORDER);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn explicit_steps_pass_through() {\n        let input = vec![Step::Check, Step::Clippy];\n\n        let resolved = resolve_steps(input.clone());\n\n        assert_eq!(resolved, input);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn feature_matrix_expands_to_five_groups() {\n        let resolved = resolve_steps(vec![Step::FeatureMatrix]);\n\n        assert_eq!(resolved.len(), 5);\n        assert_eq!(resolved[0], Step::FeatureMatrixCore);\n        assert_eq!(resolved[1], Step::FeatureMatrixI18n);\n        assert_eq!(resolved[2], Step::FeatureMatrixSubsystems);\n        assert_eq!(resolved[3], Step::FeatureMatrixLeptos);\n        assert_eq!(resolved[4], Step::FeatureMatrixDioxus);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn feature_matrix_expands_in_context() {\n        let resolved = resolve_steps(vec![Step::Fmt, Step::FeatureMatrix, Step::Coverage]);\n\n        assert_eq!(resolved.len(), 7); // 1 + 5 + 1\n        assert_eq!(resolved[0], Step::Fmt);\n        assert_eq!(resolved[6], Step::Coverage);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn pipeline_order_does_not_contain_meta_step() {\n        assert!(\n            !PIPELINE_ORDER.contains(&Step::FeatureMatrix),\n            \"PIPELINE_ORDER must not include the FeatureMatrix meta-step\"\n        );\n    }",
  "new_string": "    #[test]\n    fn empty_steps_resolve_to_pipeline_order() {\n        let resolved = resolve_steps(vec![], Profile::Full);\n\n        assert_eq!(resolved, PIPELINE_ORDER);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn explicit_steps_pass_through() {\n        let input = vec![Step::Check, Step::Clippy];\n\n        let resolved = resolve_steps(input.clone(), Profile::Full);\n\n        assert_eq!(resolved, input);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn feature_matrix_expands_to_five_groups() {\n        let resolved = resolve_steps(vec![Step::FeatureMatrix], Profile::Full);\n\n        assert_eq!(resolved.len(), 5);\n        assert_eq!(resolved[0], Step::FeatureMatrixCore);\n        assert_eq!(resolved[1], Step::FeatureMatrixI18n);\n        assert_eq!(resolved[2], Step::FeatureMatrixSubsystems);\n        assert_eq!(resolved[3], Step::FeatureMatrixLeptos);\n        assert_eq!(resolved[4], Step::FeatureMatrixDioxus);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn feature_matrix_expands_in_context() {\n        let resolved = resolve_steps(\n            vec![Step::Fmt, Step::FeatureMatrix, Step::Coverage],\n            Profile::Full,\n        );\n\n        assert_eq!(resolved.len(), 7); // 1 + 5 + 1\n        assert_eq!(resolved[0], Step::Fmt);\n        assert_eq!(resolved[6], Step::Coverage);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn pipeline_order_does_not_contain_meta_step() {\n        assert!(\n            !PIPELINE_ORDER.contains(&Step::FeatureMatrix),\n            \"PIPELINE_ORDER must not include the FeatureMatrix meta-step\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    // ── Profile / fast pipeline ───────────────────────────────────────\n\n    #[test]\n    fn profile_default_is_full() {\n        assert_eq!(Profile::default(), Profile::Full);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn empty_steps_with_fast_profile_resolve_to_fast_pipeline() {\n        let resolved = resolve_steps(vec![], Profile::Fast);\n\n        assert_eq!(resolved, FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn empty_steps_with_full_profile_resolve_to_full_pipeline() {\n        let resolved = resolve_steps(vec![], Profile::Full);\n\n        assert_eq!(resolved, PIPELINE_ORDER);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn positional_steps_override_fast_profile() {\n        // Positional steps must always win — `cargo xci --profile fast fmt`\n        // runs only `fmt`, not the fast subset.\n        let resolved = resolve_steps(vec![Step::Fmt], Profile::Fast);\n\n        assert_eq!(resolved, vec![Step::Fmt]);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn fast_pipeline_is_subset_of_full_pipeline() {\n        // Defensive: every step in the fast pipeline must also exist in the\n        // full pipeline. Catches a regression where the fast list references\n        // a step that has been removed (or renamed) from the canonical list.\n        for &step in FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER {\n            assert!(\n                PIPELINE_ORDER.contains(&step),\n                \"FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER includes {step:?} which is not in PIPELINE_ORDER\",\n            );\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn fast_pipeline_excludes_coverage_and_feature_matrix() {\n        // Guards the intent of the fast profile: the wasm-coverage recompile\n        // and the five feature-matrix groups are deliberately skipped because\n        // they are CI-only concerns that pay no marginal dividend on a\n        // pre-push gate when Unit / Integration / Adapter already ran.\n        // Catches a regression where someone reflexively re-adds them.\n        let banned = [\n            Step::Coverage,\n            Step::FeatureMatrix,\n            Step::FeatureMatrixCore,\n            Step::FeatureMatrixI18n,\n            Step::FeatureMatrixSubsystems,\n            Step::FeatureMatrixLeptos,\n            Step::FeatureMatrixDioxus,\n        ];\n\n        for forbidden in banned {\n            assert!(\n                !FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER.contains(&forbidden),\n                \"FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER must not contain {forbidden:?} — see Profile docs\",\n            );\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn fast_pipeline_does_not_contain_meta_step() {\n        // Same guard as `pipeline_order_does_not_contain_meta_step`, applied\n        // to the fast list — the meta-step is a positional-only convenience.\n        assert!(\n            !FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER.contains(&Step::FeatureMatrix),\n            \"FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER must not include the FeatureMatrix meta-step\"\n        );\n    }"
}

    Finished `dev` profile [unoptimized + debuginfo] target(s) in 0.16s
     Running `target/debug/xtask ci --help`
Run CI pipeline steps locally (alias: `cargo xci`).

`--profile full` (default) mirrors GitHub Actions exactly — all 20 gates, ~25 min locally. `--profile fast` runs the curated pre-push subset (~5 min): fmt, check, clippy, unit, integration, adapter, spec-compile-snippets, error-variant-coverage, mutual-exclusion, snapshot-count. Positional `steps` override the profile and run as-is, so `cargo xci fmt check` still works.

Usage: xtask ci [OPTIONS] [STEPS]...

Arguments:
  [STEPS]...
          Steps to run (default: every step in the selected profile).
          
          Use `feature-matrix` to run all five feature-flag groups at once. When supplied, this list overrides `--profile`.

          Possible values:
          - fmt:                       `leptosfmt --check ...`, `dx fmt --check ...`, and `cargo +nightly fmt --all --check`
          - check:                     `cargo check --workspace --all-features`
          - clippy:                    `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`
          - unit:                      Unit tests for core crates
          - i18n-browser:              Browser-executed wasm tests for ars-i18n's web-intl backend
          - dom-browser:               Browser-executed wasm tests for ars-dom's web feature
          - release:                   Release-profile compile and test smoke checks
          - integration:               Integration tests
          - adapter:                   Adapter harness tests (Leptos + Dioxus)
          - adapter-parity:            Compare per-component test counts between adapters
          - coverage:                  Generate coverage and check per-crate thresholds
          - snapshot-count:            Enforce per-component snapshot-count policy
          - spec-compile-snippets:     Parse every fenced Rust code block in the spec corpus and report any `syn::parse_file` syntax errors. Catches structural Rust bugs in spec snippets before they ship
          - error-variant-coverage:    Verify every `ComponentError` variant appears in tests
          - feature-matrix:            Meta-step: run all five feature-matrix groups
```

```
**<sub><sub>![P2 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P2-yellow?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Omit unsupported aria-readonly from group root**

When `read_only` is true, the default root is `role="group"` (and can also be `region`/`presentation`), but WAI-ARIA 1.2 does not list `aria-readonly` as supported for these roles. That means every read-only Group emits invalid ARIA that conformance tools may flag and assistive tech may ignore; the read-only state should still be propagated through `GroupContext`/`data-ars-readonly`, but `aria-readonly` belongs on descendant controls whose roles actually support it.

Useful? React with 👍 / 👎.
```

> AGENT

**Codex's finding (P2):** `aria-readonly` is not in WAI-ARIA 1.2's supported-state list for `role="group"`, `role="region"`, or `role="presentation"`. Emitting it produces invalid ARIA. Should be removed from the root; the read-only state still propagates via `GroupContext` and the `data-ars-readonly` styling hook.

This is technically correct — `aria-disabled` and `aria-invalid` are WAI-ARIA 1.2 §6.5 *global* states (valid on any role), but `aria-readonly` is *not* global; it's only valid on roles like `checkbox`, `textbox`, `combobox`, `grid`, etc. My earlier spec edit incorrectly grouped all three as "global state attributes". The fix is the right call.

Following the skill's "address comments" workflow: writing the failing test first, then making the fix, then updating spec + snapshot.

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/.t3/worktrees/ars-ui/t3code-e16f9343/crates/ars-components/src/utility/group.rs",
  "old_string": "    #[test]\n    fn root_state_attrs_emit_on_presentation_role() {\n        // WAI-ARIA 1.2 §5.4 preserves global states on `role=\"presentation\"`;\n        // ars-ui follows React Aria parity by emitting them unconditionally.\n        let attrs = Api::new(\n            Props::new()\n                .disabled(true)\n                .invalid(true)\n                .read_only(true)\n                .role(GroupRole::Presentation),\n        )\n        .root_attrs();\n\n        assert_eq!(attrs.get(&HtmlAttr::Role), Some(\"presentation\"));\n        assert_eq!(attrs.get(&HtmlAttr::Aria(AriaAttr::Disabled)), Some(\"true\"));\n        assert_eq!(attrs.get(&HtmlAttr::Aria(AriaAttr::Invalid)), Some(\"true\"));\n        assert_eq!(attrs.get(&HtmlAttr::Aria(AriaAttr::ReadOnly)), Some(\"true\"));\n    }",
  "new_string": "    #[test]\n    fn root_global_state_attrs_emit_on_presentation_role() {\n        // `aria-disabled` and `aria-invalid` are WAI-ARIA 1.2 §6.5 global\n        // states — supported on every role, including `role=\"presentation\"`,\n        // so they still emit on the root. `aria-readonly` is NOT global\n        // (see `root_read_only_emits_data_attr_but_not_aria_readonly`), so\n        // it is *not* emitted regardless of role.\n        let attrs = Api::new(\n            Props::new()\n                .disabled(true)\n                .invalid(true)\n                .read_only(true)\n                .role(GroupRole::Presentation),\n        )\n        .root_attrs();\n\n        assert_eq!(attrs.get(&HtmlAttr::Role), Some(\"presentation\"));\n        assert_eq!(attrs.get(&HtmlAttr::Aria(AriaAttr::Disabled)), Some(\"true\"));\n        assert_eq!(attrs.get(&HtmlAttr::Aria(AriaAttr::Invalid)), Some(\"true\"));\n        assert_eq!(\n            attrs.get(&HtmlAttr::Aria(AriaAttr::ReadOnly)),\n            None,\n            \"aria-readonly is non-global and must not appear on role=presentation\",\n        );\n        // The data-* styling hooks still emit for all three so CSS targeting\n        // remains symmetric.\n        assert_eq!(attrs.get(&HtmlAttr::Data(\"ars-disabled\")), Some(\"true\"));\n        assert_eq!(attrs.get(&HtmlAttr::Data(\"ars-invalid\")), Some(\"true\"));\n        assert_eq!(attrs.get(&HtmlAttr::Data(\"ars-readonly\")), Some(\"true\"));\n    }"
}

None of\n    ///   [`GroupRole`]'s variants (`group`, `region`, `presentation`) appear\n    ///   in that set, so emitting `aria-readonly` here would be invalid ARIA.\n    ///   The read-only state Descendant controls whose own roles support `aria-readonly` apply\n    ///   it themselves after reading the context.\n    ///\n    /// The three `data-ars-*` styling hooks always emit when their\n part_val);\n\n        let role_str = match self.props.role {\n            GroupRole::Group => \"group\",\n            GroupRole::Region => \"region\",\n            GroupRole::Presentation => 175	| ---- | ------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
176	| Root | `<div>` | `data-ars-scope="group"`, `data-ars-part="root"`, `role`, `aria-disabled`, `aria-invalid` |
177	
178	## 3. Accessibility
179	
180	### 3.1 ARIA Roles, States, and Properties
181	
182	| Attribute          | Element | Source            | Notes                                                        |
183	| ------------------ | ------- | ----------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------ |
184	| `role`             | Root    | `props.role`      | `"group"` (default), `"region"`, or `"presentation"`         |
185	| `aria-disabled`    | Root    | `props.disabled`  | `"true"` when the group is disabled                          |
186	| `aria-invalid`     | Root    | `props.invalid`   | `"true"` when the group is in an invalid state               |
187	| `aria-readonly`    | Root    | `props.read_only` | `"true"` when the group is read-only                         |
188	| `aria-label`       | Root    | Consumer-provided | Accessible name for the group (required for `role="region"`) |
189	| `aria-labelledby`  | Root    | Consumer-provided | Alternative accessible name via referenced element           |
190	| `aria-describedby` | Root    | Consumer-provided | Links to description or error message                        |
191	
192	- When `role` is `Region`, an accessible name (`aria-label` or `aria-labelledby`) is **required** per WAI-ARIA.
193	- State attributes (`aria-disabled`, `aria-invalid`, `aria-readonly`) are emitted on the root whenever the corresponding prop is `true`, **regardless of `role`**. WAI-ARIA 1.2 §5.4 explicitly preserves global states and properties on elements with `role="presentation"`; suppressing them there would only mask the visual state from assistive technology without changing the semantics. React Aria (parity reference, §5.1) also emits state attributes unconditionally.
194	- `aria-disabled` on a `role="group"` container does NOT natively propagate to children (unlike `<fieldset disabled>`). The adapter MUST use `GroupContext` to disable children programmatically.
195	
196	### 3.2 Context Propagation
197	
198	Adapters MUST provide `GroupContext` via the framework context system (`provide_context` in Leptos, `use_context_provider` in Dioxus). Child components that support disabling (Button, TextField, etc.) SHOULD read from `GroupContext` and merge with their own props:
199

  "old_string": "| `role`             | Root    | `props.role`      | `\"group\"` (default), `\"region\"`, or `\"presentation\"`         |\n| `aria-disabled`    | Root    | `props.disabled`  | `\"true\"` when the group is disabled                          |\n| `aria-invalid`     | Root    | `props.invalid`   | `\"true\"` when the group is in an invalid state               |\n| `aria-readonly`    | Root    | `props.read_only` | `\"true\"` when the group is read-only                         |\n| `aria-label`       | Root    | Consumer-provided | Accessible name for the group (required for `role=\"region\"`) |\n| `aria-labelledby`  | Root    | Consumer-provided | Alternative accessible name via referenced element           |\n| `aria-describedby` | Root    | Consumer-provided | Links to description or error message                        |\n\n- When `role` is `Region`, an accessible name (`aria-label` or `aria-labelledby`) is **required** per WAI-ARIA.\n- State attributes (`aria-disabled`, `aria-invalid`, `aria-readonly`) are emitted on the root whenever the corresponding prop is `true`, **regardless of `role`**. WAI-ARIA 1.2 §5.4 explicitly preserves global states and properties on elements with `role=\"presentation\"`; suppressing them there would only mask the visual state from assistive technology without changing the semantics. React Aria (parity reference, §5.1) also emits state attributes unconditionally.\n- `aria-disabled` on a `role=\"group\"` container does NOT natively propagate to children (unlike `<fieldset disabled>`). The adapter MUST use `GroupContext` to disable children programmatically.",
  "new_string": "| `role`             | Root    | `props.role`      | `\"group\"` (default), `\"region\"`, or `\"presentation\"`         |\n| `aria-disabled`    | Root    | `props.disabled`  | `\"true\"` when the group is disabled (global state)           |\n| `aria-invalid`     | Root    | `props.invalid`   | `\"true\"` when the group is in an invalid state (global state) |\n| `data-ars-disabled` | Root   | `props.disabled`  | `\"true\"` styling hook when the group is disabled             |\n| `data-ars-invalid` | Root    | `props.invalid`   | `\"true\"` styling hook when the group is in an invalid state  |\n| `data-ars-readonly` | Root   | `props.read_only` | `\"true\"` styling hook when the group is read-only            |\n| `aria-label`       | Root    | Consumer-provided | Accessible name for the group (required for `role=\"region\"`) |\n| `aria-labelledby`  | Root    | Consumer-provided | Alternative accessible name via referenced element           |\n| `aria-describedby` | Root    | Consumer-provided | Links to description or error message                        |\n\n- When `role` is `Region`, an accessible name (`aria-label` or `aria-labelledby`) is **required** per WAI-ARIA.\n- `aria-disabled` and `aria-invalid` are WAI-ARIA 1.2 §6.5 **global** states — supported on every role, including `role=\"presentation\"`. They emit on the root whenever the corresponding prop is `true`.\n- `aria-readonly` is **not** a WAI-ARIA 1.2 global state and is **not** emitted on the root. WAI-ARIA only lists `aria-readonly` as supported on roles such as `checkbox`, `textbox`, `combobox`, `grid`, `radiogroup`, `slider`, `spinbutton`, and `switch` — none of `GroupRole`'s variants (`group`, `region`, `presentation`) appear in that set, so emitting it would be invalid ARIA that conformance tools flag and assistive tech may ignore. The read-only state still propagates to descendants through `GroupContext`, and the `data-ars-readonly` styling hook still emits so CSS targeting remains symmetric. Descendant controls whose own roles support `aria-readonly` apply it themselves after reading the context.\n- `aria-disabled` on a `role=\"group\"` container does NOT natively propagate to children (unlike `<fieldset disabled>`). The adapter MUST use `GroupContext` to disable children programmatically."
}

crates/ars-components/src/utility/group.rs:593:        // so emitting `aria-readonly` on the root would be invalid ARIA that
crates/ars-components/src/utility/group.rs:599:        // `aria-readonly` apply it themselves after reading the context.

crates/ars-components/src/utility/group.rs:626:        // so they still emit on the root. `aria-readonly` is NOT global
crates/ars-components/src/utility/group.rs:644:            "aria-readonly is non-global and must not appear on role=presentation",
spec/components/utility/group.md:132:        // `aria-readonly` is non-global and is NOT emitted on the root because
spec/components/utility/group.md:198:- `aria-readonly` is **not** a WAI-ARIA 1.2 global state and is **not** emitted on the root. WAI-ARIA only lists `aria-readonly` as supported on roles such as `checkbox`, `textbox`, `combobox`, `grid`, `radiogroup`, `slider`, `spinbutton`, and `switch` — none of `GroupRole`'s variants (`group`, `region`, `presentation`) appear in that set, so emitting it would be invalid ARIA that conformance tools flag and assistive tech may ignore. The read-only state still propagates to descendants through `GroupContext`, and the `data-ars-readonly` styling hook still emits so CSS targeting remains symmetric. Descendant controls whose own roles support `aria-readonly` apply it themselves after reading the context.
```

198:- `aria-readonly` is **not** a WAI-ARIA 1.2 global state and is **not** emitted on the root. WAI-ARIA only lists `aria-readonly` as supported on roles such as `checkbox`, `textbox`, `combobox`, `grid`, `radiogroup`, `slider`, `spinbutton`, and `switch` — none of `GroupRole`'s variants (`group`, `region`, `presentation`) appear in that set, so emitting it would be invalid ARIA that conformance tools flag and assistive tech may ignore. The read-only state still propagates to descendants through `GroupContext`, and the `data-ars-readonly` styling hook still emits so CSS targeting remains symmetric. Descendant controls whose own roles support `aria-readonly` apply it themselves after reading the context.

> AGENT

Recent main CI runs all passed (6/6) — so DOM Browser isn't broadly flaky right now. But the two failing tests are animation-timing tests that don't touch any of my changed code paths. Let me hand back to you with the full picture rather than dig further unilaterally.

**Status of PR #648 (https://github.com/fogodev/ars-ui/pull/648):**

| Gate | Status |
|---|---|
| Codex 👍 | **In** — review loop complete after 3 passes (initial finding, aria-readonly fix, doc-alignment fix) |
| Threads | Both resolved with inline replies citing fix commits |
| Codex `@codex review` triggers | Posted at first push, after aria-readonly fix, and after doc fix |
| CI checks: **17 pass / 1 fail / 2 pending** | DOM Browser Tests failed; Release Verification + Coverage still running |

**DOM Browser failure details:**

Two failing tests in `crates/ars-dom`, both animation-timing tests:
- `platform::wasm_tests::animation_end_waits_for_real_completion_events`
- `platform::wasm_tests::animation_end_handles_reduced_motion_cleanup_and_transition_all`

Neither test path is in any code I touched (xtask, ars-components/group, spec docs). Diff between the green baseline (`8be9fc89`) and the failing head (`f5e9c454`) is:
1. `cb18eb01` — xci-fast infra (xtask + .cargo/config.toml + AGENTS.md + skill file)
2. `65ae1893` — `aria-readonly` removal (group.rs + group.md + snap)
3. `f5e9c454` — `Props::read_only` doc rewrite (group.rs only)

None of those reach into `ars-dom::platform`. Probable cause: headless-Chrome animation-event timing flake.

**One observation about the skill:** the Monitor's notification delivery dropped the `[change]` and `[approval]` events after the first one — its log file shows it correctly detected the approval and emitted both, but only the initial 👀 event reached the conversation. Worth noting in the skill's polling-cadence section that a single missed notification can mask completion; consider polling state directly (one quick `gh api .../reactions` call) when re-checking after a delay rather than relying solely on the monitor's notification stream.

195	### 8. Loop
196	
197	Return to step 3 (snapshot) with refreshed `seen_*` sets. Codex's next pass picks up the comment within ~30–90 s, re-adds 👀, and either approves (👍) or surfaces new findings. Step 2 (initial `@codex review` trigger) is *not* repeated — step 7 already posted the trigger for the next pass.
198	
199	## Polling cadence
200	
201	Codex review runs typically complete in 1–5 minutes for a small PR, 10–20 for a large one. Pick the cadence by what you're waiting for:
202	
203	- **First poll after push**: 60 seconds (Codex usually adds 👀 within 30–90 s).
204	- **Routine poll while 👀 is up**: 120 seconds (stays inside Anthropic's 5-min prompt cache window).
205	- **Routine poll while neither 👀 nor 👍 is present and no threads exist**: 120 seconds — Codex hasn't started yet (or finished a previous pass and a new one is pending).
206	- **👀 dropped + threads exist**: don't poll — switch immediately to the thread-handling subroutine.
207	- **No reaction at all after 5 minutes from push**: ping the user — Codex may not be configured for this repo, or the install is stalled. Also re-confirm the push actually landed.
208	- **👀 has been up >15 minutes with no thread activity**: ping the user — Codex may have stalled mid-run.
209	
210	For dynamic-paced `/loop` runs, use `ScheduleWakeup` with `delaySeconds=120` (cache-warm) until 👍 lands. Don't poll faster than 60 seconds — Codex doesn't react that quickly anyway, and the prompt cache cost dominates.
211	
212	For event-driven monitoring (one notification per state change instead of fixed-interval polling), `Monitor` works well. The script below emits a `[change]` line whenever any reaction or unresolved-thread count changes, an `[approval]` line and exits on 👍, and a `[needs_action]` line when 👀 has dropped and there is at least one unresolved Codex thread — that's the signal to stop the monitor and enter the thread-handling subroutine.
213	
214	```bash
215	prev=""
216	while true; do
217	  reactions=$(gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/issues/<pr>/reactions \
218	    --jq '.[] | "\(.user.login):\(.content)"' 2>/dev/null | sort)
219	  threads=$(gh api graphql -f query='query($o:String!,$r:String!,$n:Int!){
220	    repository(owner:$o,name:$r){pullRequest(number:$n){
221	      reviewThreads(first:100){nodes{id isResolved comments(first:1){nodes{author{login}}}}}}}}' \
222	    -F o=<owner> -F r=<repo> -F n=<pr> \
223	    --jq '.data.repository.pullRequest.reviewThreads.nodes
224	          | map(select(.isResolved==false
225	                       and (.comments.nodes[0].author.login | test("codex"; "i"))))
226	          | length' 2>/dev/null)
227	
228	  codex_eyes=$(echo "$reactions" | grep -E "codex.*:eyes$" || true)
229	  codex_thumb=$(echo "$reactions" | grep -E "codex.*:\+1$" || true)
230	
231	  cur="reactions=[$reactions] threads=$threads"
232	  [ "$cur" != "$prev" ] && echo "[change] $cur"
233	  prev=$cur
234	
235	  if [ -n "$codex_thumb" ]; then
236	    echo "[approval]"
237	    break
238	  fi
239	  if [ -z "$codex_eyes" ] && [ "$threads" -gt 0 ]; then
240	    echo "[needs_action] threads=$threads"
241	    break
242	  fi
243	
244	  sleep 120
```

247	
248	## Disagreeing with Codex
249	
250	Codex is usually right but not always. When you read a thread and your read of the spec or code says Codex is wrong (or redundant, or stylistic-without-justification):
251	
252	1. **Do not unilaterally dismiss it.** Stop and surface to the user with: what Codex says, what the spec / code says, your reasoning, and a recommendation (address as-is / push back / close-as-wontfix).
253	2. **The user picks.** Then follow through — if "push back", reply inline with the technical reasoning; if "close-as-wontfix", reply inline with the rationale before resolving; if "address", fall through to step 5 of the loop.
254	3. **Never reply inline arguing the point without checking with the user first.** Codex's threads are read by humans too — a wrong reply costs more than a one-minute clarification.
255	
256	## Anti-patterns
257	
258	- **Resolving threads without a real fix or explicit user disagreement.** Marking resolved is load-bearing — the next reviewer trusts it. An unaddressed finding hidden by a resolution is invisible until production.
259	- **Skipping the test** because "the fix is obvious." Obvious fixes regress just as often as subtle ones. The test exists to prove the fix held over time, not to prove the fix was needed today.
260	- **Replying with "fixed" only.** Quote the commit sha and a one-line summary so the thread is self-contained when read later.
261	- **Polling faster than 60 seconds.** Burns prompt cache for no benefit. Codex doesn't react that fast.
262	- **Continuing past a 👍 in case there are still threads.** 👍 means Codex is satisfied with the current state — pending threads are stale. Re-verify with the user before bulk-resolving.
263	- **Arguing with Codex inline without escalating.** A wrong reply is more expensive than a one-minute user clarification.
264	- **Hard-coding the bot login.** Different installs use different handles (`chatgpt-codex-connector`, `codex[bot]`, etc.). Match by case-insensitive substring instead.
265	
266	## What this skill is not
```

  "new_string": "**Prefer Bash `run_in_background` over Monitor for this loop.** The verdict we\nwait for is a single terminal state (👍 approval, or 👀-dropped with new\nfindings) — exactly the \"one notification when the condition is true\" shape\nBash `run_in_background` is built for. Each iteration of the loop has exactly\none terminal condition to watch, so one completion notification per loop pass\nis what we want.\n\nMonitor is designed for ongoing event streams (one notification per\nstate-change line) — useful when you want to know about every transition in\nreal time, but notification delivery is best-effort and intermediate events\ncan drop. Real-world example from this skill's own bootstrap session: the 👀\n→ 👍 transition fired correctly inside the monitor and was written to the\nscript's stdout file, but the follow-up `[change]` and `[approval]`\nnotifications never reached the conversation. }

Re-fetch PR state directly:\n   ```bash\n   gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/issues/<pr>/reactions \\\n     --jq '.[] | \"\\(.user.login):\\(.content)\"'\n   ```\n   If the output shows `:+1` (👍), or shows 👀 absent with unresolved Codex\n   threads, the run completed and you missed the signal. Re-run the\n   threads query and act on the verdict.\n\nThe general principle: **the script's stdout file is the source of truth\nfor detection; the notification stream is best-effort delivery.** Treat\nthe two as separate channels — the file is durable, the channel is lossy."

  "command": "git add .agents/skills/waiting-for-codex-review/SKILL.md && git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\ndocs(skill): waiting-for-codex-review — prefer run_in_background, add defensive re-check\n\nTwo follow-ups discovered while running the skill end-to-end on PR #648.\n\n1. **Notification delivery is best-effort.** The polling Monitor correctly\n   detected the 👀 → 👍 transition and wrote `[change]` + `[approval]` to\n   its output file, but only the very first `[change]` reached the\n   conversation as a notification. The model sat waiting for an event that\n   had already fired ~30 minutes earlier. Add a new \"Defensive re-check\"\n   subsection under Polling cadence with the explicit rule: after a quiet\n   period >5 min since the last event (or >10 min total wait once 👀 is\n   seen), `Read` the task's output file *and* re-fetch reactions directly\n   via `gh api .../reactions` before assuming the run is ongoing. The\n   stdout file is durable; the notification stream is lossy.\n\n2. **Prefer Bash `run_in_background` over Monitor.** The verdict the loop\n   waits for is a single terminal state (👍 approval, or 👀-dropped with\n   new findings) — exactly the \"one notification when condition is true\"\n   shape Bash `run_in_background` is built for. Monitor is for ongoing\n   event streams (every state change becomes a notification), useful when\n   you want real-time visibility but exposed to the same dropped-event\n   problem we just hit. Reframe the recommended snippet to exit with\n   `VERDICT=approval` or `VERDICT=needs_action threads=N` on its last\n   stdout line; one `Read` at completion recovers the verdict.\n\nAnti-patterns updated to match: replaced \"Treating Monitor's\nnotification stream as a complete event log\" with the tool-neutral\n\"Treating background-task notifications as a complete event log\" (applies\nto both Bash run_in_background and Monitor); added a new anti-pattern\n\"Using Monitor when you only need a terminal verdict.\"\n\nNo behavioural changes elsewhere — pure documentation update inside\nthe skill file. Verified via `cargo xci --profile fast` on the warm\ncache (all 10 steps passed).\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -5; echo \"---PUSH---\"; git push 2>&1 | tail -3",
  "description": "Commit + push skill update",
  "timeout": 120000
}

  "command": "prev=\"\"\nwhile true; do\n  reactions=$(gh api repos/fogodev/ars-ui/issues/648/reactions \\\n    --jq '.[] | \"\\(.user.login):\\(.content)\"' 2>/dev/null | sort)\n  threads=$(gh api graphql -f query='query($o:String!,$r:String!,$n:Int!){\n    repository(owner:$o,name:$r){pullRequest(number:$n){\n      reviewThreads(first:100){nodes{id isResolved comments(first:1){nodes{author{login}}}}}}}}' \\\n    -F o=fogodev -F r=ars-ui -F n=648 \\\n    --jq '.data.repository.pullRequest.reviewThreads.nodes\n          | map(select(.isResolved==false\n                       and (.comments.nodes[0].author.login | test(\"codex\"; \"i\"))))\n          | length' 2>/dev/null)\n\n  codex_eyes=$(echo \"$reactions\" | grep -E \"codex.*:eyes$\" || true)\n  codex_thumb=$(echo \"$reactions\" | grep -E \"codex.*:\\+1$\" || true)\n\n  cur=\"reactions=[$reactions] threads=$threads\"\n  if [ \"$cur\" != \"$prev\" ]; then\n    echo \"[change] $cur\"\n  fi\n  prev=$cur\n\n  if [ -n \"$codex_thumb\" ]; then\n    echo \"VERDICT=approval\"\n    exit 0\n  fi\n  if [ -z \"$codex_eyes\" ] && [ \"$threads\" -gt 0 ]; then\n    echo \"VERDICT=needs_action threads=$threads\"\n    exit 0\n  fi\n\n  sleep 120\ndone",
  "description": "Codex re-review poll (updated skill pattern)",
  "timeout": 1500000,
  "run_in_background": true
}
